Signal legend:
- `axiom`: a new axiom: trusted outright
- `sorry`: an unproved obligation; in tiers 0-2 it is an axiom
- `opaque`: an opaque constant: a value nobody can inspect
- `native`: trusts native code (forbidden by README)
- `implemented_by`: runtime behaviour differs from the definition
- `unsafe`: Lean unsafe definition
- `partial`: partial def: an opaque constant in disguise
- `assume`: a model assumption: must be unreachable in Go (README, Model assumptions)
- `angelic`: an angelic exit: the model refuses to continue (sound only if Go cannot reach it)
- `panic`: a (stuck) run-time panic in the model
- `step-rule`: a semantics rule or unfolding (any form: `⤳`, `is_go_step_pure`, `IsGoStepPureDet`, `FuncUnfold`, ...; in tier 0 also a changed line inside one)
- `semantics-class`: a semantics class, or an instance of one (or of the contexts defining `is_go_step_pure`)
- `adequacy`: an adequacy theorem or its parameters
- `trusted-impl`: a hand-written model of Go code
- `trusted-list`: goose configuration: what is trusted/translated/axiomatized
- `skip-list`: goose configuration: package variables not translated at all (trusted: not running each initializer is unobservable)
- `global-var-addr`: the address of a package variable by its literal name: the one way hand-written code can reach a variable goose skips (the name has no Lean declaration)
- `extension`: an extension's semantics (new primitive operations)
- `set_option`: an option change (linters off, max heartbeats, ...)
- `verbatim`: goose emits Lean text verbatim
- `model-table`: goose configuration: a Go model overlay (model Go replaces or adds to the real package; R12)
- `model-replaces`: goose configuration: which real declarations a Go model replaces, or which methods it may add to real types (R12)
- `gomodel-replace`: a Go model `init` that replaces a real `init.N` (R12)
- `gomodel-choice`: a choice or stuck point in a Go model: needs a reason (review-trusted)

#### `fmt.Fprintf` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go:63):

```go
// MODEL: Fprintf is Go's, but for one line: Go's p.doPrintf(format, a) is the model printer's
// doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, false), which appends to p.buf what Go appends up to its first
// method call or directive the model does not format, and arbitrary strings from there, before
// each method call and at the end (see the file comment for what the model printer covers and
// where it is stuck; TestModelFprintf checks the bytes Write receives and the methods called;
// TestModelAlias a method reading p.buf through a slice an earlier Write kept). The printer, its
// pool (ppFree), the one w.Write(p.buf) and p.free() are Go's own.
func Fprintf(w io.Writer, format string, a ...any) (n int, err error) {
	p := newPrinter()
	doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, false)
	n, err = w.Write(p.buf)
	p.free()
	return
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src fmt.Fprintf`):

```go
package fmt // import "fmt"

// Fprintf formats according to a format specifier and writes to w.
// It returns the number of bytes written and any write error encountered.
func Fprintf(w io.Writer, format string, a ...any) (n int, err error) {
	p := newPrinter()
	p.doPrintf(format, a)
	n, err = w.Write(p.buf)
	p.free()
	return
}
```

#### `fmt.errorf` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go:40):

```go
// MODEL: errorf is Go's, but for its printer: Go's p.doPrintf(format, a), with p.wrapErrs set, is
// the model printer's doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, true), which appends the message to p.buf (Go's,
// with arbitrary strings from the first directive it does not format) and gives the indices of the
// %w arguments (or that they are unknown); errorfResult builds the error from them as Go's switch
// on len(p.wrappedErrs) does: errors.New, a *wrapError, a *wrapErrors (see the file comment for
// what the model printer covers and where it is stuck; TestModelErrorf compares the error's kind,
// message and wrapped errors with Go's). The printer is Go's own, from newPrinter and back to the
// pool with p.free(), and the message is read back from p.buf as Go reads it: the pool is
// observable outside fmt, since Fprintf hands p.buf to a writer, which may keep the slice and see
// what a later errorf writes into the same printer's buffer, or write into it itself
// (TestModelAlias, goose/model/fmt).
func errorf(format string, a ...any) error {
	if len(a) == 0 && stringslite.IndexByte(format, '%') == -1 {
		return nil
	}
	p := newPrinter()
	wrapped, unknown := doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, true)
	s := string(p.buf)
	err := errorfResult(s, wrapped, unknown, a)
	p.free()
	return err
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src fmt.errorf`):

```go
package fmt // import "fmt"

// Errorf formats according to a format specifier and returns the string as a
// value that satisfies error.
//
// If the format specifier includes a %w verb with an error operand,
// the returned error will implement an Unwrap method returning the operand.
// If there is more than one %w verb, the returned error will implement an
// Unwrap method returning a []error containing all the %w operands in the
// order they appear in the arguments.
// It is invalid to supply the %w verb with an operand that does not implement
// the error interface. The %w verb is otherwise a synonym for %v.
func Errorf(format string, a ...any) (err error) {
	// This function has been split in a somewhat unnatural way
	// so that both it and the errors.New call can be inlined.
	if err = errorf(format, a...); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	// No formatting was needed. We can avoid some allocations and other work.
	// See https://go.dev/cl/708836 for details.
	return errors.New(format)
}
// errorf formats and returns an error value, or nil if no formatting is required.
func errorf(format string, a ...any) error {
	if len(a) == 0 && stringslite.IndexByte(format, '%') == -1 {
		return nil
	}
	p := newPrinter()
	p.wrapErrs = true
	p.doPrintf(format, a)
	s := string(p.buf)
	var err error
	switch len(p.wrappedErrs) {
	case 0:
		err = errors.New(s)
	case 1:
		w := &wrapError{msg: s}
		w.err, _ = a[p.wrappedErrs[0]].(error)
		err = w
	default:
		if p.reordered {
			slices.Sort(p.wrappedErrs)
		}
		var errs []error
		for i, argNum := range p.wrappedErrs {
			if i > 0 && p.wrappedErrs[i-1] == argNum {
				continue
			}
			if e, ok := a[argNum].(error); ok {
				errs = append(errs, e)
			}
		}
		err = &wrapErrors{s, errs}
	}
	p.free()
	return err
}
```

#### `fmt.pp.doPrint` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go:90):

```go
// MODEL: pp.doPrint prints the operands under %v, a space between two that are not strings. Go
// tells a string by reflect.TypeOf(arg).Kind() == reflect.String (reflection, which is stuck) and
// prints each with p.printArg, which is stuck on the operands of the file comment; Fprint's spec
// admits them (fmt.printArgOk). The model calls of each operand what Go's printArg calls under %v
// (visitArg: its Error() or String(), stuck where the model printer is), after appending arbitrary
// strings to p.buf (writeArbitrary), which stand for what Go has appended by then (a method may
// read p.buf through a slice a writer kept), and appends arbitrary strings at the end, for the
// rest of Go's output (TestModelFprint checks the calls, TestModelAlias the buffer). Fprint and
// Sprint, Go's own, print with it.
func (p *pp) doPrint(a []any) {
	for _, x := range a {
		writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
		visitArg(x, 'v', false, false)
	}
	writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src fmt.pp.doPrint`):

```go
package fmt // import "fmt"

func (p *pp) doPrint(a []any) {
	prevString := false
	for argNum, arg := range a {
		isString := arg != nil && reflect.TypeOf(arg).Kind() == reflect.String
		// Add a space between two non-string arguments.
		if argNum > 0 && !isString && !prevString {
			p.buf.writeByte(' ')
		}
		p.printArg(arg, 'v')
		prevString = isString
	}
}
```

#### `fmt.pp.doPrintln` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go:107):

```go
// MODEL: pp.doPrintln is doPrint's model (see there): Go's doPrintln, for each operand, appends a
// space if it is not the first and prints it with p.printArg(arg, 'v'), then appends '\n'; the
// model calls of each operand what printArg calls under %v (visitArg), after arbitrary strings
// (writeArbitrary), which cover Go's space and what printArg appended before, and ends with
// arbitrary strings, which cover the rest and the newline (TestModelFprintln checks the calls).
// Fprintln and Sprintln, Go's own, print with it.
func (p *pp) doPrintln(a []any) {
	for _, x := range a {
		writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
		visitArg(x, 'v', false, false)
	}
	writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src fmt.pp.doPrintln`):

```go
package fmt // import "fmt"

// doPrintln is like doPrint but always adds a space between arguments
// and a newline after the last argument.
func (p *pp) doPrintln(a []any) {
	for argNum, arg := range a {
		if argNum > 0 {
			p.buf.writeByte(' ')
		}
		p.printArg(arg, 'v')
	}
	p.buf.writeByte('\n')
}
```

#### `internal/bytealg.MakeNoZero` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/bytealg.go:8):

```go
// MODEL: MakeNoZero(n) is a slice of length n and capacity at least n whose
// bytes, up to the capacity, are not zeroed. Go's has no Go body (goose:
// "external function"): the runtime defines it (runtime/slice.go,
// `//go:linkname bytealg_MakeNoZero internal/bytealg.MakeNoZero`, which the
// overlay blanks so that gc links this body instead) as
//
//	if uintptr(len) > maxAlloc {
//		panicmakeslicelen()
//	}
//	cap := roundupsize(uintptr(len), true)
//	return unsafe.Slice((*byte)(mallocgc(cap, nil, false)), cap)[:len]
//
// The model chooses the capacity (gomodel.ChooseUint64: any c >= n, which
// includes the size class roundupsize rounds n up to) and each byte up to the
// capacity (gomodel.ChooseUint64: mallocgc with needzero false returns memory
// with whatever it held, which a Go program cannot predict). A negative n
// panics in make with Go's message ("makeslice: len out of range"). An n
// above maxAlloc (2^48), where gc panics, is not checked: the model
// allocates, as the semantics' make and append do (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md,
// "No limit on allocation size", which names MakeNoZero).
func MakeNoZero(n int) []byte {
	c := int(gomodel.ChooseUint64() >> 1)
	if c < n {
		c = n
	}
	b := make([]byte, n, c)
	full := b[:cap(b)]
	for i := range full {
		full[i] = byte(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
	}
	return b
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src internal/bytealg.MakeNoZero`):

```go
package bytealg // import "internal/bytealg"

// MakeNoZero makes a slice of length n and capacity of at least n Bytes
// without zeroing the bytes (including the bytes between len and cap).
// It is the caller's responsibility to ensure uninitialized bytes
// do not leak to the end user.
func MakeNoZero(n int) []byte
```

#### `internal/gomodel.ChooseString` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/internal/gomodel/choose.go:3):

```go
// ChooseString is an arbitrary string: every string is a result (and the loop
// may also not terminate). It is translated Go over Choose and ChooseUint64,
// not a primitive: a model uses it where Go's value is outside what the model
// tracks (a linker-set string, a symbol name, a command-line argument). In Go
// it is "".
func ChooseString() string {
	var b []byte
	for Choose() {
		b = append(b, byte(ChooseUint64()))
	}
	return string(b)
}
```

// ChooseStrings is an arbitrary []string (nil or not, of any length, every

// os.runtime_args and syscall.runtime_envs return append([]string{}, ...),
// never nil). It is translated Go over Choose and ChooseString. In Go it is

func ChooseStrings() []string {
	var a []string
	if Choose() {
		a = []string{}
	}

}

- `[model]` before: none

#### `internal/sync.Mutex.Lock` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go:13):

```go
// MODEL: Go's Lock is a compare-and-swap of state from 0 to mutexLocked, and
// on failure lockSlow, which spins and parks on the runtime's semaphore
// (runtime_SemacquireMutex, runtime_canSpin, runtime_doSpin,
// runtime_nanotime, all without a Go body) and records waiters, wakeups and
// starvation in state, read with plain loads that race with the
// compare-and-swaps. Goose translates lockSlow, but the hooks have no body and
// the racy reads are outside what the sequentially consistent semantics
// models; and the protocol in state is shared with TryLock and Unlock, so
// they are replaced together. The model keeps state at 0 (unlocked) or
// mutexLocked and spins on the fast path's compare-and-swap until it
// succeeds: Lock returns exactly when it takes the mutex, as in Go; a
// spinning model goroutine where Go's parks is a schedule with the same
// results (the model has no fairness and no starvation mode, which only
// order waiters). A goroutine that never gets the mutex spins forever where
// Go's parks forever: spinning for parking is partial correctness, and Go's
// deadlock detector (a global deadlock becomes the fatal throw "all
// goroutines are asleep") is not modelled (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md).
func (m *Mutex) Lock() {
	for !atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, 0, mutexLocked) {
	}
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src internal/sync.Mutex.Lock`):

```go
package sync // import "internal/sync"

// Lock locks m.
//
// See package [sync.Mutex] documentation.
func (m *Mutex) Lock() {
	// Fast path: grab unlocked mutex.
	if atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, 0, mutexLocked) {
		if race.Enabled {
			race.Acquire(unsafe.Pointer(m))
		}
		return
	}
	// Slow path (outlined so that the fast path can be inlined)
	m.lockSlow()
}
```

#### `internal/sync.Mutex.TryLock` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go:35):

```go
// MODEL: Go's TryLock returns false if state is locked or starving, or if its
// compare-and-swap of state to locked loses to a concurrent change, else
// true. The model's state has no starvation bit and no waiters, so where Go
// fails on an unlocked mutex (starvation mode, or a waiter changing state),
// the model fails by an arbitrary choice (gomodel.Choose); otherwise it is the
// compare-and-swap of 0 to mutexLocked. It thus returns false in every case
// Go does, and true only when it took the mutex, as in Go.
func (m *Mutex) TryLock() bool {
	if gomodel.Choose() {
		return false
	}
	return atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, 0, mutexLocked)
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src internal/sync.Mutex.TryLock`):

```go
package sync // import "internal/sync"

// TryLock tries to lock m and reports whether it succeeded.
//
// See package [sync.Mutex] documentation.
func (m *Mutex) TryLock() bool {
	old := m.state
	if old&(mutexLocked|mutexStarving) != 0 {
		return false
	}

	// There may be a goroutine waiting for the mutex, but we are
	// running now and can try to grab the mutex before that
	// goroutine wakes up.
	if !atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, old, old|mutexLocked) {
		return false
	}

	if race.Enabled {
		race.Acquire(unsafe.Pointer(m))
	}
	return true
}
```

#### `internal/sync.Mutex.Unlock` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go:49):

```go
// MODEL: Go's Unlock subtracts mutexLocked from state and, if waiters remain,
// wakes one (unlockSlow, through runtime_Semrelease); on an unlocked mutex it
// calls fatal("sync: unlock of unlocked mutex"). The model's state has no
// waiters (a spinning Lock sees the 0), so Unlock is the compare-and-swap of
// mutexLocked to 0, and fatal where it fails, that is where the mutex is not
// locked.
func (m *Mutex) Unlock() {
	if !atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, mutexLocked, 0) {
		fatal("sync: unlock of unlocked mutex")
	}
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src internal/sync.Mutex.Unlock`):

```go
package sync // import "internal/sync"

// Unlock unlocks m.
//
// See package [sync.Mutex] documentation.
func (m *Mutex) Unlock() {
	if race.Enabled {
		_ = m.state
		race.Release(unsafe.Pointer(m))
	}

	// Fast path: drop lock bit.
	new := atomic.AddInt32(&m.state, -mutexLocked)
	if new != 0 {
		// Outlined slow path to allow inlining the fast path.
		// To hide unlockSlow during tracing we skip one extra frame when tracing GoUnblock.
		m.unlockSlow(new)
	}
}
```

#### `internal/sync.fatal` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go:61):

```go
// MODEL: Go's fatal (a runtime push linkname, no Go body) prints the message
// and ends the program, and recover cannot catch it. The model has no
// behaviour there (gomodel.Stuck): a verified program never calls it.
func fatal(string) {
	gomodel.Stuck()
}
```

#### `log.Printf` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/log/log.go:12):

```go
// MODEL: Printf is std.output(0, 2, func(b []byte) []byte { return
// fmt.Appendf(b, format, v...) }). The standard logger std is not translated
// (its initializer is New(os.Stderr, "", LstdFlags), and os.Stderr is not a
// File in the model: perennial#663), nor is any function that changes it
// (SetOutput, SetFlags, SetPrefix, Default, Logger's setters), so in every
// execution of the model std is as New made it: flags LstdFlags, no prefix,
// output os.Stderr. Its output method then:
//   - returns at once if the output is io.Discard (l.isDiscard), which needs
//     a SetOutput no execution reaches; the model has both outcomes anyway
//     (gomodel.Choose: it formats or does nothing);
//   - reads the clock (time.Now), the prefix and the flags (atomic loads), and
//     with LstdFlags not the caller's file and line: no effect;
//   - takes a buffer from bufferPool, formats the date and time into it, and
//     calls the closure, which formats the arguments as fmt.Appendf does:
//     fmt's printer and doPrintf, which call the arguments' Error, String and
//     Format methods, the only effects on the program. The model formats them
//     with fmt.Fprintf, which runs the same newPrinter, doPrintf and free;
//   - appends a newline, takes std.outMu (held only by output, never while
//     the arguments' methods run) and writes the bytes to standard error,
//     ignoring the result: an effect outside the program, which the model
//     drops, as it drops Fprintf's bytes (modelDiscard). The write can also
//     block (a full stderr pipe, holding std.outMu) or end the process
//     (EPIPE on fd 2: os.File.Write's epipecheck raises SIGPIPE): both stop
//     the program there, so their traces are prefixes of the model's, which
//     returns (partial correctness, as for an exit).
//
// Neither can panic other than through the arguments' methods.
func Printf(format string, v ...any) {
	if gomodel.Choose() {
		fmt.Fprintf(modelDiscard{}, format, v...)
	}
}
```

}

#### `runtime.Frames.Next` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/runtime/symtab.go:8):

```go
// MODEL: ci.Next() is the next frame of the program counters ci.callers,
// symbolized through the binary's tables (findfunc, the inlining tree, the
// cgo symbolizer; goose: "inc or dec statement with unsupported type
// uintptr", symtab.go:131), and whether another one follows. Go reads and
// writes *ci, so a nil ci panics (nil dereference) and a concurrent call on
// the same Frames races; the model's load and store of ci.callers do both.
// The model stores ci.callers on every call, also on an exhausted Frames,
// where Go only reads *ci: two concurrent Next calls on an exhausted Frames
// race in the model and not in Go (extra behaviour, the safe direction: a
// race is stuck in GooseLang, so a proof must exclude it). The model leaves *ci unchanged, where Go advances it, which no caller
// observes (the fields are unexported). It does not read the words
// ci.callers points to, which Go does (ci.callers[0]): a concurrent write to
// them races in Go and not in the model, but Go reads them only to look them
// up (any value gives some frames, a garbage one is skipped), and the model's
// frames are arbitrary anyway, so no outcome of that race is missing.
// Every exported field of the frame and more is arbitrary, a superset of
// what Go returns (goose/model/runtime/callers_test.go: a nil Func for a frame
// of a function inlined into its caller, Function/File/Line of the call
// sites, the zero frame with more false once the frames are exhausted). A
// non-nil Func is new(Func): Func is zero-size, and the semantics allocates a
// zero-size value at an arbitrary non-null address that owns no memory, so
// two results can be equal or not and a proof can read nothing through it, as
// with Go's pointers into the binary's tables. startLine and Entry are
// arbitrary too; funcInfo stays zero (unexported, read only by the runtime).
func (ci *Frames) Next() (frame Frame, more bool) {
	ci.callers = ci.callers
	frame.PC = uintptr(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
	if gomodel.Choose() {
		frame.Func = new(Func)
	}
	frame.Function = gomodel.ChooseString()
	frame.File = gomodel.ChooseString()
	frame.Line = int(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
	frame.startLine = int(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
	frame.Entry = uintptr(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
	more = gomodel.Choose()
	return
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src runtime.Frames.Next`):

```go
package runtime // import "runtime"

// Next returns a [Frame] representing the next call frame in the slice
// of PC values. If it has already returned all call frames, Next
// returns a zero [Frame].
//
// The more result indicates whether the next call to Next will return
// a valid [Frame]. It does not necessarily indicate whether this call
// returned one.
//
// See the [Frames] example for idiomatic usage.
func (ci *Frames) Next() (frame Frame, more bool) {
	for len(ci.frames) < 2 {
		// Find the next frame.
		// We need to look for 2 frames so we know what
		// to return for the "more" result.
		if len(ci.callers) == 0 {
			break
		}
		var pc uintptr
		if ci.nextPC != 0 {
			pc, ci.nextPC = ci.nextPC, 0
		} else {
			pc, ci.callers = ci.callers[0], ci.callers[1:]
		}
		funcInfo := findfunc(pc)
		if !funcInfo.valid() {
			if cgoSymbolizerAvailable() {
				// Pre-expand cgo frames. We could do this
				// incrementally, too, but there's no way to
				// avoid allocation in this case anyway.
				ci.frames = append(ci.frames, expandCgoFrames(pc)...)
			}
			continue
		}
		f := funcInfo._Func()
		entry := f.Entry()
		// We store the pc of the start of the instruction following
		// the instruction in question (the call or the inline mark).
		// This is done for historical reasons, and to make FuncForPC
		// work correctly for entries in the result of runtime.Callers.
		// Decrement to get back to the instruction we care about.
		//
		// It is not possible to get pc == entry from runtime.Callers,
		// but if the caller does provide one, provide best-effort
		// results by avoiding backing out of the function entirely.
		if pc > entry {
			pc--
		}
		// It's important that interpret pc non-strictly as cgoTraceback may
		// have added bogus PCs with a valid funcInfo but invalid PCDATA.
		u, uf := newInlineUnwinder(funcInfo, pc)
		sf := u.srcFunc(uf)
		if u.isInlined(uf) {
			// Note: entry is not modified. It always refers to a real frame, not an inlined one.
			// File/line from funcline1 below are already correct.
			f = nil

			// When CallersFrame is invoked using the PC list returned by Callers,
			// the PC list includes virtual PCs corresponding to each outer frame
			// around an innermost real inlined PC.
			// We also want to support code passing in a PC list extracted from a
			// stack trace, and there only the real PCs are printed, not the virtual ones.
			// So check to see if the implied virtual PC for this PC (obtained from the
			// unwinder itself) is the next PC in ci.callers. If not, insert it.
			// The +1 here correspond to the pc-- above: the output of Callers
			// and therefore the input to CallersFrames is return PCs from the stack;
			// The pc-- backs up into the CALL instruction (not the first byte of the CALL
			// instruction, but good enough to find it nonetheless).
			// There are no cycles in implied virtual PCs (some number of frames were
			// inlined, but that number is finite), so this unpacking cannot cause an infinite loop.
			for unext := u.next(uf); unext.valid() && len(ci.callers) > 0 && ci.callers[0] != unext.pc+1; unext = u.next(unext) {
				snext := u.srcFunc(unext)
				if snext.funcID == abi.FuncIDWrapper && elideWrapperCalling(sf.funcID) {
					// Skip, because tracebackPCs (inside runtime.Callers) would too.
					continue
				}
				ci.nextPC = unext.pc + 1
				break
			}
... (38 more lines)
```

#### `strings.Split` (changed)

Model (goose/model/overlay/strings/strings.go:11):

```go
// MODEL: Split(s, sep) is the substrings of s between the occurrences of sep
// (found left to right, not overlapping), as Go's genSplit(s, sep, 0, -1)
// computes them. The model finds the occurrences by testing HasPrefix at each
// position, where Go's genSplit counts them with Count and finds them with
// Index. Goose translates the real Split, but at len(s) = 2^63-1 (a string gc
// cannot hold) genSplit's len(s)+1 overflows and it panics, so its spec would
// need a bound on len(s) that Split's spec does not have
// (Perennial/Code/strings.toml). The model returns the same fields as
// genSplit wherever genSplit does not panic, and there only: building such a
// string needs an allocation gc refuses, which the semantics allows
// (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md, "No limit on allocation size"). For an empty
// sep, Go splits s into its UTF-8 sequences (explode); the model is stuck
// there, so every spec of Split requires sep to be non-empty.
func Split(s, sep string) []string {
	if len(sep) == 0 {
		gomodel.Stuck()
	}
	var a []string
	var field string
	rest := s
	for len(rest) > 0 {
		if HasPrefix(rest, sep) {
			a = append(a, field)
			field = ""
			rest = rest[len(sep):]
			continue
		}
		field += rest[:1]
		rest = rest[1:]
	}
	return append(a, field)
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src strings.Split`):

```go
package strings // import "strings"

// Split slices s into all substrings separated by sep and returns a slice of
// the substrings between those separators.
//
// If s does not contain sep and sep is not empty, Split returns a
// slice of length 1 whose only element is s.
//
// If sep is empty, Split splits after each UTF-8 sequence. If both s
// and sep are empty, Split returns an empty slice.
//
// It is equivalent to [SplitN] with a count of -1.
//
// To split around the first instance of a separator, see [Cut].
func Split(s, sep string) []string { return genSplit(s, sep, 0, -1) }
```

#### `sync.Pool.Get` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/sync/pool.go:75):

```go
// MODEL: Go's Get pins the goroutine to its P and takes the P's private
// value, else pops its shared chain, else steals from other Ps and the
// victim cache (getSlow); if it found nothing and New is not nil, it returns
// New(). Goose translates it but is stuck as for Put. The model's search
// takes an arbitrary element of the pool's state or nothing, even when the
// state is not empty (gomodel.Choose: Go's search may miss values in other
// Ps' chains or dropped by the GC, and gomodel.ChooseUint64: which value it
// finds); the rest, calling New, is Go's.
func (p *Pool) Get() any {
	s := poolModelOf(p)
	var x any
	s.mu.Lock()
	n := uint64(len(s.items))
	if n != 0 && !gomodel.Choose() {
		i := gomodel.ChooseUint64() % n
		x = s.items[i]
		s.items[i] = s.items[n-1]
		s.items = s.items[:n-1]
	}
	s.mu.Unlock()
	if x == nil && p.New != nil {
		x = p.New()
	}
	return x
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src sync.Pool.Get`):

```go
package sync // import "sync"

// Get selects an arbitrary item from the [Pool], removes it from the
// Pool, and returns it to the caller.
// Get may choose to ignore the pool and treat it as empty.
// Callers should not assume any relation between values passed to [Pool.Put] and
// the values returned by Get.
//
// If Get would otherwise return nil and p.New is non-nil, Get returns
// the result of calling p.New.
func (p *Pool) Get() any {
	if race.Enabled {
		race.Disable()
	}
	l, pid := p.pin()
	x := l.private
	l.private = nil
	if x == nil {
		// Try to pop the head of the local shard. We prefer
		// the head over the tail for temporal locality of
		// reuse.
		x, _ = l.shared.popHead()
		if x == nil {
			x = p.getSlow(pid)
		}
	}
	runtime_procUnpin()
	if race.Enabled {
		race.Enable()
		if x != nil {
			race.Acquire(poolRaceAddr(x))
		}
	}
	if x == nil && p.New != nil {
		x = p.New()
	}
	return x
}
```

#### `sync.Pool.Put` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/sync/pool.go:52):

```go
// MODEL: Go's Put ignores nil and otherwise pins the goroutine to its P and
// stores x in that P's private slot or pushes it onto the P's lock-free
// shared chain; the GC later moves the per-P pools to the victim cache and
// then drops them. Goose translates it, but the translation is stuck at
// indexLocal's conversion of a uintptr back to an unsafe.Pointer (pointer
// arithmetic) and at poolDequeue's store of an any through an
// unsafe.Pointer to its eface; runtime_procPin, which keeps the goroutine on
// its P, has no Go body. The model ignores nil as Go does, and otherwise
// adds x to the pool's state or drops it (gomodel.Choose: the GC may drop it
// in Go before any Get).
func (p *Pool) Put(x any) {
	if x == nil {
		return
	}
	s := poolModelOf(p)
	if gomodel.Choose() {
		return
	}
	s.mu.Lock()
	s.items = append(s.items, x)
	s.mu.Unlock()
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src sync.Pool.Put`):

```go
package sync // import "sync"

// Put adds x to the pool.
func (p *Pool) Put(x any) {
	if x == nil {
		return
	}
	if race.Enabled {
		if runtime_randn(4) == 0 {
			// Randomly drop x on floor.
			return
		}
		race.ReleaseMerge(poolRaceAddr(x))
		race.Disable()
	}
	l, _ := p.pin()
	if l.private == nil {
		l.private = x
	} else {
		l.shared.pushHead(x)
	}
	runtime_procUnpin()
	if race.Enabled {
		race.Enable()
	}
}
```

#### `sync.runtime_Semacquire` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/sync/runtime.go:13):

```go
// MODEL: Go's runtime_Semacquire (runtime/sema.go, semacquire1) waits until
// *s > 0 and decrements it, trying first
//
//	func cansemacquire(addr *uint32) bool {
//		for {
//			v := atomic.Load(addr)
//			if v == 0 {
//				return false
//			}
//			if atomic.Cas(addr, v, v-1) {
//				return true
//			}
//		}
//	}
//
// and, while that fails, parking on a queue that runtime_Semrelease wakes. The
// model is cansemacquire's loop, retried until it succeeds: it returns exactly
// when it has decremented a positive *s, as Go's does; a model goroutine
// spinning where Go's parks is a schedule with the same results (the queue
// only orders the waiters). A goroutine that never acquires spins forever
// where Go's parks forever: spinning for parking is partial correctness, and
// Go's deadlock detector (a global deadlock becomes the fatal throw "all
// goroutines are asleep") is not modelled (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md).
func runtime_Semacquire(s *uint32) {
	for {
		v := atomic.LoadUint32(s)
		if v == 0 {
			continue
		}
		if atomic.CompareAndSwapUint32(s, v, v-1) {
			return
		}
	}
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src sync.runtime_Semacquire`):

```go
package sync // import "sync"

// Semacquire waits until *s > 0 and then atomically decrements it.
// It is intended as a simple sleep primitive for use by the synchronization
// library and should not be used directly.
func runtime_Semacquire(s *uint32)
```

#### `sync.runtime_notifyListNotifyAll` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/sync/runtime.go:104):

```go
// MODEL: Go's runtime_notifyListNotifyAll (notifyListNotifyAll) returns if
// l.wait == l.notify (no waiter), and otherwise, under the list's lock, sets
// l.notify to l.wait and readies every parked waiter. The model reads both and
// sets l.notify with a compare-and-swap, retried if l.notify changed in
// between: the store linearizes at the compare-and-swap, as Go's does under
// the lock (the lock only orders the notify calls, which the compare-and-swap
// also does). The waiters are readied by the new l.notify (see
// runtime_notifyListWait).
func runtime_notifyListNotifyAll(l *notifyList) {
	for {
		n := atomic.LoadUint32(&l.notify)
		w := atomic.LoadUint32(&l.wait)
		if n == w || atomic.CompareAndSwapUint32(&l.notify, n, w) {
			return
		}
	}
}
```

// MODEL: Go's runtime_notifyListNotifyOne (notifyListNotifyOne) returns if
// l.wait == l.notify (no waiter), and otherwise, under the list's lock,
// increments l.notify and readies the waiter holding the old value as its
// ticket, if it has parked yet (if not, its Wait sees the ticket notified and
// returns at once). The model is the same increment by compare-and-swap,
// retried if l.notify changed in between (as for NotifyAll).
func runtime_notifyListNotifyOne(l *notifyList) {
	for {
		n := atomic.LoadUint32(&l.notify)
		if n == atomic.LoadUint32(&l.wait) || atomic.CompareAndSwapUint32(&l.notify, n, n+1) {
			return
		}
	}
}
```

#### `syscall.prlimit1` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/syscall/prlimit.go:9):

```go
// MODEL: prlimit1 is the prlimit64 system call: Go's body is
// RawSyscall6(SYS_PRLIMIT64, uintptr(pid), uintptr(resource),
// uintptr(unsafe.Pointer(newlimit)), uintptr(unsafe.Pointer(old)), 0, 0) and,
// for a non-zero errno, errnoErr(e1). The conversions of pointers to uintptr
// and the system call (assembly) have no semantics. The kernel reads
// *newlimit if newlimit is non-nil, writes *old if old is non-nil and the call
// succeeds, and returns 0 or an errno; it cannot fault on Go's pointers and
// cannot panic. The model reads *newlimit (so a concurrent write races, as
// with the kernel's read), may write arbitrary limits to *old (a superset of
// the kernel's writes: always on success, never on failure), and returns
// errnoErr of an arbitrary errno, nil for 0, as Go's stub does with the
// kernel's. The limits themselves, the process's state, are not modelled.
func prlimit1(pid int, resource int, newlimit *Rlimit, old *Rlimit) (err error) {
	if newlimit != nil {
		_ = *newlimit
	}
	if old != nil && gomodel.Choose() {
		old.Cur = gomodel.ChooseUint64()
		old.Max = gomodel.ChooseUint64()
	}
	e1 := Errno(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
	if e1 != 0 {
		err = errnoErr(e1)
	}
	return
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src syscall.prlimit1`):

```go
package syscall // import "syscall"

func prlimit1(pid int, resource int, newlimit *Rlimit, old *Rlimit) (err error) {
	_, _, e1 := RawSyscall6(SYS_PRLIMIT64, uintptr(pid), uintptr(resource), uintptr(unsafe.Pointer(newlimit)), uintptr(unsafe.Pointer(old)), 0, 0)
	if e1 != 0 {
		err = errnoErr(e1)
	}
	return
}
```

#### `time.After` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go:86):

```go
// MODEL: Go's After(d) is NewTimer(d).C: a timer that is never stopped or
// reset, whose channel delivers one value once the runtime clock reaches
// when(d). The model is NewTimer's (makeTimerChan, a firing that sends
// blocking, sendTimes), over the model's runtime clock (runtimeClock), which
// the proofs can speak of: when(d) is computed (whenAt) from a reading of that
// clock, and the firing goroutine waits until the clock reaches it
// (runtimeClockWaitUntil). The model's claim on the clock: the readings of
// Go's runtime clock form a non-decreasing sequence of positive int64s
// (runtimeNano), and the model's clock has every such sequence; Go's timer
// fires only once the clock has reached when.
func After(d Duration) <-chan Time {
	c := makeTimerChan()
	w := whenAt(runtimeClockNow(), d)
	checkWhen(w, 0)
	go func() {
		runtimeClockWaitUntil(w)
		sendTimes(c, 0, 0)
	}()
	return c
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src time.After`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// After waits for the duration to elapse and then sends the current time
// on the returned channel.
// It is equivalent to [NewTimer](d).C.
//
// Before Go 1.23, this documentation warned that the underlying
// [Timer] would not be recovered by the garbage collector until the
// timer fired, and that if efficiency was a concern, code should use
// NewTimer instead and call [Timer.Stop] if the timer is no longer needed.
// As of Go 1.23, the garbage collector can recover unreferenced,
// unstopped timers. There is no reason to prefer NewTimer when After will do.
func After(d Duration) <-chan Time {
	return NewTimer(d).C
}
```

#### `time.NewTicker` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/tick.go:12):

```go
// MODEL: Go's NewTicker is
//
//	if d <= 0 {
//		panic("non-positive interval for NewTicker")
//	}
//	c := make(chan Time, 1)
//	t := (*Ticker)(unsafe.Pointer(newTimer(when(d), int64(d), sendTime, c, syncTimer(c))))
//	t.C = c
//	return t
//
// The model keeps the panic and is NewTimer's model otherwise (the channel,
// sendTimes), with the runtime's timer allocated as a Ticker
// (newRuntimeTicker) in place of the conversion, and armed with period d.
func NewTicker(d Duration) *Ticker {
	if d <= 0 {
		panic("non-positive interval for NewTicker")
	}
	c := makeTimerChan()
	w := when(d)
	fire := timerFunc(sendTimes, c)
	checkWhen(w, int64(d))
	t := newRuntimeTicker(fire)
	startTimer(fire, w, int64(d))
	t.C = c
	return t
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src time.NewTicker`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// NewTicker returns a new [Ticker] containing a channel that will send
// the current time on the channel after each tick. The period of the
// ticks is specified by the duration argument. The ticker will adjust
// the time interval or drop ticks to make up for slow receivers.
// The duration d must be greater than zero; if not, NewTicker will
// panic.
//
// Before Go 1.23, the garbage collector did not recover
// tickers that had not yet expired or been stopped, so code often
// immediately deferred t.Stop after calling NewTicker, to make
// the ticker recoverable when it was no longer needed.
// As of Go 1.23, the garbage collector can recover unreferenced
// tickers, even if they haven't been stopped.
// The Stop method is no longer necessary to help the garbage collector.
// (Code may of course still want to call Stop to stop the ticker for other reasons.)
func NewTicker(d Duration) *Ticker {
	if d <= 0 {
		panic("non-positive interval for NewTicker")
	}
	// Give the channel a 1-element time buffer.
	// If the client falls behind while reading, we drop ticks
	// on the floor until the client catches up.
	c := make(chan Time, 1)
	t := (*Ticker)(unsafe.Pointer(newTimer(when(d), int64(d), sendTime, c, syncTimer(c))))
	t.C = c
	return t
}
```

#### `time.NewTimer` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go:63):

```go
// MODEL: Go's NewTimer is
//
//	c := make(chan Time, 1)
//	t := newTimer(when(d), 0, sendTime, c, syncTimer(c))
//	t.C = c
//	return t
//
// syncTimer(c) is non-nil unless GODEBUG asynctimerchan=1, and then the
// runtime makes c synchronous: cap(c) and len(c) are 0, and a receive after a
// firing gets a value made at the receive. GooseLang cannot change a channel's
// kind after make, so the model makes c either way (makeTimerChan), and fires
// sendTimes in place of sendTime: sendTime's non-blocking send would drop the
// value on an unbuffered c, where the runtime delivers it at the next receive.
// sendTimes sends blocking, so a receive gets the value whenever it comes (or
// a later one: the model also allows values after Stop or Reset, which Go's
// synchronous channels never deliver).
func NewTimer(d Duration) *Timer {
	c := makeTimerChan()
	t := newTimer(when(d), 0, sendTimes, c, nil)
	t.C = c
	return t
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src time.NewTimer`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// NewTimer creates a new Timer that will send
// the current time on its channel after at least duration d.
//
// Before Go 1.23, the garbage collector did not recover
// timers that had not yet expired or been stopped, so code often
// immediately deferred t.Stop after calling NewTimer, to make
// the timer recoverable when it was no longer needed.
// As of Go 1.23, the garbage collector can recover unreferenced
// timers, even if they haven't expired or been stopped.
// The Stop method is no longer necessary to help the garbage collector.
// (Code may of course still want to call Stop to stop the timer for other reasons.)
//
// Before Go 1.23, the channel associated with a Timer was
// asynchronous (buffered, capacity 1), which meant that
// stale time values could be received even after [Timer.Stop]
// or [Timer.Reset] returned.
// As of Go 1.23, the channel is synchronous (unbuffered, capacity 0),
// eliminating the possibility of those stale values.
//
// The GODEBUG setting asynctimerchan=1 restores both pre-Go 1.23
// behaviors: when set, unexpired timers won't be garbage collected, and
// channels will have buffered capacity. This setting may be removed
// in Go 1.27 or later.
func NewTimer(d Duration) *Timer {
	c := make(chan Time, 1)
	t := newTimer(when(d), 0, sendTime, c, syncTimer(c))
	t.C = c
	return t
}
```

#### `time.Ticker.Reset` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/tick.go:55):

```go
// MODEL: Go's Reset is
//
//	if d <= 0 {
//		panic("non-positive interval for Ticker.Reset")
//	}
//	if !t.initTicker {
//		panic("time: Reset called on uninitialized Ticker")
//	}
//	resetTimer((*Timer)(unsafe.Pointer(t)), when(d), int64(d))
//
// The model is the same with resetTimer's model at t's address
// (resetTimerAt) in place of the conversion.
func (t *Ticker) Reset(d Duration) {
	if d <= 0 {
		panic("non-positive interval for Ticker.Reset")
	}
	if !t.initTicker {
		panic("time: Reset called on uninitialized Ticker")
	}
	resetTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t), when(d), int64(d))
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src time.Ticker.Reset`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// Reset stops a ticker and resets its period to the specified duration.
// The next tick will arrive after the new period elapses. The duration d
// must be greater than zero; if not, Reset will panic.
func (t *Ticker) Reset(d Duration) {
	if d <= 0 {
		panic("non-positive interval for Ticker.Reset")
	}
	if !t.initTicker {
		panic("time: Reset called on uninitialized Ticker")
	}
	resetTimer((*Timer)(unsafe.Pointer(t)), when(d), int64(d))
}
```

#### `time.Ticker.Stop` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/tick.go:39):

```go
// MODEL: Go's Stop is
//
//	if !t.initTicker {
//		return
//	}
//	stopTimer((*Timer)(unsafe.Pointer(t)))
//
// The model is the same with stopTimer's model at t's address (stopTimerAt)
// in place of the conversion, which is the identity on the address.
func (t *Ticker) Stop() {
	if !t.initTicker {
		return
	}
	stopTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t))
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src time.Ticker.Stop`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// Stop turns off a ticker. After Stop, no more ticks will be sent.
// Stop does not close the channel, to prevent a concurrent goroutine
// reading from the channel from seeing an erroneous "tick".
func (t *Ticker) Stop() {
	if !t.initTicker {
		// This is misuse, and the same for time.Timer would panic,
		// but this didn't always panic, and we keep it not panicking
		// to avoid breaking old programs. See issue 21874.
		return
	}
	stopTimer((*Timer)(unsafe.Pointer(t)))
}
```

#### `time.newTimer` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go:26):

```go
// MODEL: the runtime's newTimer (runtime/time.go) allocates a timer
// (runtime.timeTimer: the Timer's fields, then the runtime's state), sets
// initTimer, and arms it: once the runtime clock reaches when it calls
// f(arg, seq, delay) from the timer goroutine, and again every period if
// period != 0. The arming (timer.modify) throws if when <= 0 or period < 0;
// the model is stuck there (startTimer). Its channel argument cp only makes the
// timer's channel synchronous; NewTimer's and NewTicker's models pass nil and
// choose the channel's kind themselves (makeTimerChan), and AfterFunc passes
// nil. The model calls f with arbitrary seq and delay, so it covers the
// runtime's.
func newTimer(when, period int64, f func(any, uintptr, int64), arg any, cp unsafe.Pointer) *Timer {
	fire := timerFunc(f, arg)
	checkWhen(when, period)
	t := newRuntimeTimer(fire)
	startTimer(fire, when, period)
	return t
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src time.newTimer`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// NewTimer creates a new Timer that will send
// the current time on its channel after at least duration d.
//
// Before Go 1.23, the garbage collector did not recover
// timers that had not yet expired or been stopped, so code often
// immediately deferred t.Stop after calling NewTimer, to make
// the timer recoverable when it was no longer needed.
// As of Go 1.23, the garbage collector can recover unreferenced
// timers, even if they haven't expired or been stopped.
// The Stop method is no longer necessary to help the garbage collector.
// (Code may of course still want to call Stop to stop the timer for other reasons.)
//
// Before Go 1.23, the channel associated with a Timer was
// asynchronous (buffered, capacity 1), which meant that
// stale time values could be received even after [Timer.Stop]
// or [Timer.Reset] returned.
// As of Go 1.23, the channel is synchronous (unbuffered, capacity 0),
// eliminating the possibility of those stale values.
//
// The GODEBUG setting asynctimerchan=1 restores both pre-Go 1.23
// behaviors: when set, unexpired timers won't be garbage collected, and
// channels will have buffered capacity. This setting may be removed
// in Go 1.27 or later.
func NewTimer(d Duration) *Timer {
	c := make(chan Time, 1)
	t := newTimer(when(d), 0, sendTime, c, syncTimer(c))
	t.C = c
	return t
}
// The arg cp is a chan Time, but the declaration in runtime uses a pointer,
// so we use a pointer here too. This keeps some tools that aggressively
// compare linknamed symbol definitions happier.
//
//go:linkname newTimer
func newTimer(when, period int64, f func(any, uintptr, int64), arg any, cp unsafe.Pointer) *Timer
```

#### `time.resetTimer` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go:54):

```go
// MODEL: the runtime's resetTimer re-arms t (when, period: the arming throws
// as newTimer's does) and reports whether it was armed. The model loads t's
// runtime state as stopTimer does, is stuck where Go throws, starts one more
// firing goroutine with the state's call (startTimer) and returns an arbitrary
// bool. The earlier firings may still happen (as if Reset came later).
func resetTimer(t *Timer, when, period int64) bool {
	return resetTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t), when, period)
}
```

#### `time.runtimeNano` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/time.go:25):

```go
// MODEL: the runtime's runtimeNano (time_runtimeNano) is the bubble's fake
// clock inside a testing/synctest bubble, else nanotime(), the monotonic
// clock that now() also returns as mono. Both are positive: on Linux nanotime
// is CLOCK_MONOTONIC, the time since boot, and a bubble's clock starts at
// 2000-01-01 UTC; `when` relies on it ("runtimeNano() and d are always
// positive"), and the runtime's timers throw on a non-positive when. The model
// returns now()'s mono, with a non-positive one (which no Go run reads)
// replaced by 1, so that its readings are positive as Go's are and the
// models' timers can keep the runtime's throws (startTimer).
func runtimeNano() int64 {
	_, _, mono := now()
	if mono <= 0 {
		mono = 1
	}
	return mono
}
```

Real (`go doc -u -src time.runtimeNano`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// runtimeNano returns the current value of the runtime clock in nanoseconds.
// When called within a synctest.Run bubble, it returns the group's fake clock.
//
//go:linkname runtimeNano
func runtimeNano() int64
```

// MODEL: the runtime's runtimeNow (runtime/time.go, time_runtimeNow) is

//	}
//	return time_now()

// is any (sec, nsec, mono); so it covers the bubble's fake clock too.
func runtimeNow() (sec int64, nsec int32, mono int64) {

#### `time.stopTimer` (new)

Model (goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go:44):

```go
// MODEL: the runtime's stopTimer stops the runtime's timer t and reports
// whether it stopped it before it fired. The model loads t's runtime state
// (stuck if t is not one of the runtime's timers: a Timer the program
// allocated, or a copy, where Go reads memory that is not a timer) and returns
// an arbitrary bool; it does not stop the timer, whose firings may still
// happen (each one is a schedule of Go's in which Stop came later).
func stopTimer(t *Timer) bool {
	return stopTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t))
}
```

package primitive // import "github.com/goose-lang/primitive"

// Assume lets the proof assume that `c` is true.
//
// In Go, if the assumption is violated this function will panic, whereas in the
// GooseLang model it will loop infinitely.
func Assume(c bool) {
	if !c {
		panic("Assume condition violated")
	}
}
```

### Perennial/TrustedCode/time.lean: `now.impl`

(the docstring quotes no Go code: the model must be read as GooseLang)

Real Go source (`go doc -src time.now`):

```go
package time // import "time"

// Now returns the current local time.
func Now() Time {
	sec, nsec, mono := runtimeNow()
	if mono == 0 {
		return Time{uint64(nsec), sec + unixToInternal, Local}
	}
	mono -= startNano
	sec += unixToInternal - minWall
	if uint64(sec)>>33 != 0 {
		// Seconds field overflowed the 33 bits available when
		// storing a monotonic time. This will be true after
		// March 16, 2157.
		return Time{uint64(nsec), sec + minWall, Local}
	}
	return Time{hasMonotonic | uint64(sec)<<nsecShift | uint64(nsec), mono, Local}
}
```

## Diffs

### Perennial/Code/fmt.toml  (tier 0, goose-config)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/Code/fmt.toml b/Perennial/Code/fmt.toml
index 166e74b52..a391af4db 100644
--- a/Perennial/Code/fmt.toml
+++ b/Perennial/Code/fmt.toml
@@ -1,24 +1,34 @@
-# The print path of print.go and format.go (`Sprintf` and the printer it runs), the package
-# variables, and errors.go's types. Not translated (stuck when called):
+# The print path of print.go and format.go (`Sprintf`, `Fprint`, `Errorf` and the printer they run),
+# the package variables, and errors.go's types. Not translated (stuck when called):
 # - `pp.printValue`, which formats by reflection (`reflect` has no semantics), and
 #   `pp.fmtComplex`, which needs the `real`/`imag` built-ins goose does not support;
 # - scan.go (only its package variables are translated, for the initializer);
-# - `errorf`, translated with `Errorf` at stage 2 of perennial#671, and `Println`, which writes
-#   to `os.Stdout`, with `Printf` and `Print` at stage 2.
+# - `Print` and `Println`, which write to `os.Stdout` (the model of os has none).
+# Go models (goose/model/overlay/fmt, [model] below): `errorf`, `Fprintf`, `pp.doPrint` and `pp.doPrintln` print
+# with the model printer (printmodel.go) where the real print path is stuck. `Printf` is trusted
+# (TrustedCode/fmt.lean, as on lean): it formats with the model's `printfFormat` and returns an
+# arbitrary count and error for the write to `os.Stdout` (translating the write needs a semantics
+# primitive for an arbitrary interface value, its error).
 translate = [
   "*",
   "!pp.printValue", "!pp.fmtComplex",
-  "!errorf", "!Println",
-  # trusted (below)
-  "!Errorf", "!Fprint", "!Fprintf", "!Print", "!Printf",
+  "!Print", "!Println", "!Printf",
+  # the primitive stubs (trusted, below)
+  "!underlyingAs", "!kindIs",
   # scan.go: only the package variables
   "!Scan", "!Scanln", "!Scanf", "!Sscan", "!Sscanln", "!Sscanf", "!Fscan", "!Fscanln", "!Fscanf",
   "!isSpace", "!notSpace", "!newScanState", "!indexRune", "!hasX", "!hexDigit", "!errorHandler",
   "!ss.*", "!readRune.*", "!stringReader.*", "!scanError.*",
 ]
-imports = ["!*", "io", "errors", "sync", "unicode/utf8", "reflect", "strconv"]
-# the trusted models of TrustedCode/fmt.lean, until stage 2 of perennial#671
-trusted = ["Errorf", "Fprint", "Fprintf", "Print", "Printf"]
+imports = ["!*", "io", "errors", "sync", "unicode/utf8", "reflect", "strconv", "internal/stringslite",
+  "internal/gomodel"]
+# the model's primitive stubs `underlyingAs` (goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go) and `kindIs`
+# (printmodel.go), and `Printf`, modelled in TrustedCode/fmt.lean
+trusted = ["underlyingAs", "kindIs", "Printf"]
 
 # translate the definitions of all type declarations goose supports
 translate_types = true
+
+[model]
+dir = "goose/model/overlay/fmt"
+replaces = ["errorf", "Fprintf", "pp.doPrint", "pp.doPrintln"]
```

### Perennial/Code/internal/bytealg.toml  (tier 0, goose-config)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/Code/internal/bytealg.toml b/Perennial/Code/internal/bytealg.toml
index 075bf4ce7..ae663d013 100644
--- a/Perennial/Code/internal/bytealg.toml
+++ b/Perennial/Code/internal/bytealg.toml
@@ -1,26 +1,15 @@
-# Only MakeNoZero (strings.Builder's grow): a `go:linkname` to the runtime with no Go body
-# ("external function"), trusted (TrustedCode/internal/bytealg.lean).
-# `MaxLen`, which the package's initializer (`init.0`) sets; `LastIndexByteString` (path.Split,
-# path.Base), whose only body is the generic Go loop in lastindexbyte_generic.go (no build tag, no
-# assembly on any architecture).
+# `MaxLen`, which the package's initializer (`init.0`, a Go model below) sets;
+# `LastIndexByteString` (path.Split, path.Base), whose only body is the generic Go loop in
+# lastindexbyte_generic.go (no build tag, no assembly on any architecture).
 # For strings.LastIndex, strings.Index and strings.Cut (through internal/stringslite): the generic
 # Go of bytealg.go (`PrimeRK`, `HashStr`, `HashStrRev`, `IndexRabinKarp`), amd64's `MaxBruteForce`
 # and `Cutover` (index_amd64.go), and `IndexByteString` and `IndexString`, whose bodies on amd64 are
 # assembly: they are translated from their Go models (the [model] table below). For strings.Count:
-# `CountString`, assembly on amd64 too, translated from its Go model.
+# `CountString`, assembly on amd64 too, translated from its Go model. `MakeNoZero` (strings.Builder's
+# grow) and `init.0` (sets `MaxLen`), from their Go models too.
 translate = ["MaxLen", "LastIndexByteString", "PrimeRK", "HashStr", "HashStrRev", "IndexRabinKarp",
-  "MaxBruteForce", "Cutover", "IndexByteString", "IndexString", "CountString"]
+  "MaxBruteForce", "Cutover", "IndexByteString", "IndexString", "CountString", "MakeNoZero", "init.0"]
 imports = ["!*", "internal/gomodel"]
-# `init.0` sets `MaxLen` from `cpu.X86.HasAVX2`: trusted, as one of the two values
-# (TrustedCode/internal/bytealg.lean). Goose translates `init.0` itself, to a read of the global
-# `internal/cpu.X86`, which no package initializer sets: the runtime does (`cpu.Initialize`, from
-# `schedinit`, with `cpuid` in assembly). The irreducible unit is that variable's value; trusting
-# it instead is not possible with goose as it is: a `trusted` variable with no initializer
-# expression (`var X86 struct{...}`) gets neither a `GlobalAlloc` nor an `X86.init` call in
-# `initialize'` (goose.go, the package initializer), so nothing could model the runtime filling it,
-# and translating it leaves it zero (MaxLen always 31: more defined than gc). Pending a goose
-# change that gives such a variable an `X.init` hook.
-trusted = ["MakeNoZero", "init.0"]
 
 # The Go models of the four bodyless declarations of indexbyte_native.go and index_native.go,
 # assembly on amd64 (indexbyte_amd64.s, index_amd64.s): `IndexByte`/`IndexByteString` are Go's own
@@ -38,7 +27,13 @@ trusted = ["MakeNoZero", "init.0"]
 # count_generic.go, verbatim (count.go). (`Compare`, bytes.Compare's, has no Go body, and
 # compare_amd64.s, which defines it, also defines `runtime.cmpstring`, which gc's string
 # comparisons call, so that file cannot be blanked: bytes.Compare is modelled in its package.)
+# `MakeNoZero` (bytealg.go): no Go body ("external function"); the runtime defines it
+# (runtime/slice.go, a `go:linkname` push, which the overlay blanks); the model chooses the
+# capacity and the bytes, bytealg.go. `init.0` (index_amd64.go) reads
+# `internal/cpu.X86`, which only the runtime fills (from `cpuid`): translated, X86 stays zero and
+# MaxLen is always 31, more defined than gc; the model chooses 63 or 31, init.go.
 [model]
 dir = "goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg"
-replaces = ["IndexByte", "IndexByteString", "Index", "IndexString", "CompareString", "Count", "CountString"]
+replaces = ["IndexByte", "IndexByteString", "Index", "IndexString", "CompareString", "Count", "CountString",
+  "MakeNoZero", "init.0"]
 blank_asm = ["indexbyte_amd64.s", "index_amd64.s", "count_amd64.s"]
```

### Perennial/Code/runtime.toml  (tier 0, goose-config)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/Code/runtime.toml b/Perennial/Code/runtime.toml
index d3450fc11..471d61e3f 100644
--- a/Perennial/Code/runtime.toml
+++ b/Perennial/Code/runtime.toml
@@ -1,13 +1,28 @@
 # `Caller` is translated with its body: `callers` and `CallersFrames(...).Next()`, below.
 translate = ["!*", "Version", "Caller", "Callers", "CallersFrames"]
 
-imports = ["!*", "internal/runtime/atomic", "internal/runtime/sys"]
-
-# `Gosched` (TrustedCode/runtime.lean); `buildVersion`, which `Version` returns and the linker
-# sets (TrustedCode/runtime.lean: its `init` stores an arbitrary string); `callers`, the stack walk
-# `Callers` and `Caller` call, and `Frames.Next`, the symbolization of program counters (TrustedCode/runtime.lean:
-# arbitrary words, arbitrary frames)
-trusted = ["Gosched", "buildVersion", "callers", "Frames.Next"]
+imports = ["!*", "internal/runtime/atomic", "internal/runtime/sys", "internal/gomodel"]
 
 # translate the definitions of all type declarations goose supports
 translate_types = true
+
+# `callers`, the stack walk `Callers` calls (`getg`, `sys.GetCallerPC`: compiler intrinsics), is
+# trusted (TrustedCode/runtime.lean): its model is `callersModel`, a Go function the overlay adds
+# (arbitrary words), which `callers.impl` calls. Not a replacement, so that gc runs the real stack
+# walk (the testing package panics on an empty one).
+# `buildVersion`, which `Version` returns and the linker sets: trusted, `buildVersion.init` stores
+# the opaque `buildVersionValue` (TrustedCode/runtime.lean), which a client's premise about the
+# build can name (an overlay model would make it an arbitrary choice no premise can refer to).
+# `Gosched` (`mcall(gosched_m)`: assembly and the scheduler): trusted, `Gosched.impl` returns. Not an
+# overlay replacement: a replacement would also replace it for the runtime's own callers in gc builds
+# (`runtime.GC` yields with `Gosched` while it waits for the sweep), so a model body that does not yield
+# hangs gc's GC; trusted, gc keeps the real one.
+trusted = ["Gosched", "callers", "buildVersion"]
+
+# The Go model (goose/model/overlay/runtime): each declaration's MODEL: comment says why it has at
+# least Go's behaviours. `Frames.Next`, the
+# symbolization of program counters (the binary's tables; arbitrary frames); and the addition
+# `callersModel` (above).
+[model]
+dir = "goose/model/overlay/runtime"
+replaces = ["Frames.Next"]
```

### Perennial/Code/strings.toml  (tier 0, goose-config)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/Code/strings.toml b/Perennial/Code/strings.toml
index 596ed7e08..910c530a4 100644
--- a/Perennial/Code/strings.toml
+++ b/Perennial/Code/strings.toml
@@ -1,7 +1,7 @@
 # The real Go is translated: Builder (but three methods, modelled), Join (over Builder), Compare
 # (over `bytealg.CompareString`, modelled in its package), HasPrefix and TrimPrefix (over
-# internal/stringslite's), and the rest of `translate`. `grow`'s `bytealg.MakeNoZero` is trusted in
-# its package. `TrimLeft` with its ASCII helpers (`trimLeftByte`, `makeASCIISet`,
+# internal/stringslite's), and the rest of `translate`. `grow`'s `bytealg.MakeNoZero` is a Go
+# model in its package. `TrimLeft` with its ASCII helpers (`trimLeftByte`, `makeASCIISet`,
 # `asciiSet.contains`, `trimLeftASCII`); its non-ASCII path, `trimLeftUnicode`, is not translated
 # (it reaches `IndexRune` and utf8 decoding): a call of `TrimLeft` with a cutset that is not all ASCII
 # has no proof. `LastIndex`, `IndexByte` and `Cut` (over internal/stringslite and internal/bytealg).
@@ -20,7 +20,13 @@ translate_types = true
 # least Go's behaviours. `Builder.String` (`unsafe.String`, which aliases the buffer),
 # `Builder.copyCheck` (an `unsafe.Pointer` converted to `*Builder`, which the semantics has no rule
 # for) and `Builder.Reset` (which must not store to the `addr` that `copyCheck`'s model makes
-# read-only); `Split` (`Count` and `Index`, which end in `internal/bytealg`'s assembly); `IndexRune`
+# read-only); `Split`. Goose translates the real `Split` (`genSplit`, `Count`, `Index`, `explode`),
+# but its spec could not keep today's statement: for any `s` with `len(s) = 2^63-1` the real
+# `genSplit(s, sep, 0, -1)` panics (`len(s)+1` overflows to `-2^63`, so `n > len(s)+1` holds for
+# every `n >= 1`, `n` becomes `-2^63` and `make([]string, n)` panics), and string values are not
+# bounded in the semantics (`len` only gives `< 2^63`), so the spec would need a new premise
+# `len(s) < 2^63-1`. gc cannot hold such a string (maxAlloc is 2^48). The model has no such
+# arithmetic; it is stuck on an empty `sep`. `IndexRune`
 # (its search for a non-ASCII rune is written with goto, which goose does not translate).
 [model]
 dir = "goose/model/overlay/strings"
```

 ]

-
-axiomatize = ["notifyList"]
+imports = ["sync/atomic", "internal/race", "internal/synctest", "internal/sync", "internal/gomodel"]
 
+# The Go model (goose/model/overlay/sync): each declaration's MODEL: comment says why it has at least
+# Go's behaviours. The runtime hooks (`runtime_Semacquire*`, `runtime_Semrelease`, `runtime_notifyList*`,
+# `runtime_registerPoolCleanup`: no Go body, defined by the runtime's push linknames) and

-  "runtime_notifyList*",

-  # `copyChecker` is a `uintptr` holding its own address, and `check` converts

+  # `copyChecker` is a `uintptr` holding its own address, and `check` converts `unsafe.Pointer(c)` to
+  # `uintptr` and CASes it; the semantics has neither conversion, so both are modelled in
+  # TrustedCode/sync.lean (as an `unsafe.Pointer`). A Go model would change an unexported type's
+  # kind, which the overlay's API check refuses (follow-up).
   "copyChecker",
   "copyChecker.*",
-
-  "runtime_Semacquire*",
-  "runtime_Semrelease",
-
-  # a runtime linkname with no Go body
-  "runtime_registerPoolCleanup",
-
-  # `Pool.Get` and `Put` are modeled in TrustedCode/sync.lean with the Go model
-  # goose/model/pool. With them and all their helpers (`pin`, `pinSlow`,
-  # `getSlow`, `indexLocal`, `poolChain.*`, `poolDequeue.*`) in `translate`,
-  # goose rejects only the runtime linknames with no Go body
-  # (`runtime_procPin`, `runtime_procUnpin`, `runtime_LoadAcquintptr`,
-  # `runtime_StoreReluintptr`), but what it translates is irreducible too:

-  # the plain accesses to `l.private` are race-free only because `procPin`
-  # keeps the goroutine on its P, which Go code cannot express.
-  "Pool.Get",
-  "Pool.Put",
 ]
 
 # translate the definitions of all type declarations goose supports
 translate_types = true
+
+[model]

### Perennial/Code/syscall.toml  (tier 0, goose-config)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/Code/syscall.toml b/Perennial/Code/syscall.toml
index 9c2e0b7a8..023d38f35 100644
--- a/Perennial/Code/syscall.toml
+++ b/Perennial/Code/syscall.toml
@@ -1,12 +1,14 @@
-# syscall: the types, and the package-level variable initializers (what importing syscall runs).
-# Two pieces have no Go body or no modelled effect; they are trusted models
-# (TrustedCode/syscall.lean):
-# * `runtime_envs` (`env_unix.go`, a `go:linkname` into the runtime): an arbitrary `[]string`;
-# * `init.0` (`rlimit.go`: raises the soft RLIMIT_NOFILE through the raw `prlimit` system call):
-#   a no-op. The changed limit is only observable by `exec` (and `Getrlimit`), which Perennial
-#   does not model (the user's decision, fix-semantics Case 33);
-# * `Exit` (`syscall.go`, a body-less declaration the runtime provides, `syscall_Exit`): the
-#   process exits, `AngelicExit`.
+# syscall: the types, and the package-level variable initializers and `init` (what importing
+# syscall runs). `init.0` (`rlimit.go`: raises the soft RLIMIT_NOFILE to its hard limit minus one,
+# and records the original in `origRlimitNofile`) is translated, with `Getrlimit` and `setrlimit`,
+# over the model of the system call `prlimit1` (below). Trusted models (TrustedCode/syscall.lean) of
+# two body-less declarations the runtime provides (go:linkname pushes): `runtime_envs`
+# (`env_unix.go`): an arbitrary `[]string`, a call of `internal/gomodel.ChooseStrings`; `Exit`
+# (`syscall.go`, `syscall_Exit`): the process exits, `AngelicExit`.
+# `Setenv`, `Unsetenv`, `Clearenv` and the runtime's hooks `runtimeSetenv`/`runtimeUnsetenv`/
+# `runtimeClearenv` stay untranslated (a call is stuck). Keep it so, or make them stuck on the key
+# "GODEBUG": gc's runtime re-reads GODEBUG there, and the Go models that read a GODEBUG setting once
+# (time's `makeTimerChan`; docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md, "time") rely on it not changing mid-run.
 translate = ["!*",
   "envs", "env", "copyenv",
   "none", "slash", "forceClone3", "fcntl64Syscall",
@@ -14,12 +16,20 @@ translate = ["!*",
   "Stdin", "Stdout", "Stderr",
   "errEAGAIN", "errEINVAL", "errENOENT",
   "errors", "signals", "_",
+  "init.0", "origRlimitNofile", "Getrlimit", "setrlimit", "adjustFileLimit", "errnoErr",
   # the constants these initializers use
-  "SYS_FCNTL", "EAGAIN", "EINVAL", "ENOENT",
+  "SYS_FCNTL", "EAGAIN", "EINVAL", "ENOENT", "RLIMIT_NOFILE",
 ]
-trusted = ["runtime_envs", "init.0", "Exit"]
+trusted = ["runtime_envs", "Exit"]
 
-imports = ["!*", "sync"]
+imports = ["!*", "sync", "sync/atomic", "internal/gomodel"]
 
 # translate the definitions of all type declarations goose supports
 translate_types = true
+
+# The Go model (goose/model/overlay/syscall): each declaration's MODEL: comment says why it has at
+# least Go's behaviours. `prlimit1` (`zsyscall_linux_amd64.go`, the `prlimit64` system call through `RawSyscall6` of `uintptr(unsafe.Pointer(...))` arguments): reads
+# `*newlimit`, may write arbitrary limits to `*old`, returns an arbitrary errno's error.
+[model]
+dir = "goose/model/overlay/syscall"
+replaces = ["prlimit1"]
```

### Perennial/Code/time.toml  (tier 0, goose-config)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/Code/time.toml b/Perennial/Code/time.toml
index c6bbfb23b..0fa2c1f35 100644
--- a/Perennial/Code/time.toml
+++ b/Perennial/Code/time.toml
@@ -1,30 +1,10 @@
-imports = ["!*", "errors"]
-trusted = [
-  # TODO: these are for verifying (rather than modeling) `After`, but the proof
-  # would be blocked by the lack of support for type assertions (needed in time.sendTime)
-  "runtimeNano", # go:linkname runtimeNano
-  "newTimer",    # go:linkname newTimer
-  "syncTimer",
-  "runtimeIsBubbled", # go:linkname runtimeIsBubbled (true only inside a synctest bubble)
-  "runtimeNow",  # go:linkname runtimeNow (the wall clock and the monotonic clock)
-  "stopTimer",   # go:linkname stopTimer
-  "resetTimer",  # go:linkname resetTimer
-  # "sendTime",
+imports = ["!*", "errors", "sync/atomic", "internal/gomodel"]
+# Primitive stubs (Perennial/TrustedCode/time.lean): `now` and `Sleep` are bodyless functions the
+# runtime defines (gc links them to the runtime's clock and sleep); `newRuntimeTimer`,
+# `newRuntimeTicker` and `runtimeTimerFire` are additions of the model (goose/model/overlay/time),
+# the runtime's state of a timer.
+trusted = ["now", "Sleep", "newRuntimeTimer", "newRuntimeTicker", "runtimeTimerFire"]
 
-  "After",
-  "Sleep",
-
-  # the runtime makes their channel synchronous (cap 0), which goose's `make` cannot express
-  "NewTimer",
-  "NewTicker",
-  # `(*Timer)(unsafe.Pointer(t))` is stuck: `Timer` is not plain
-  "Ticker.Stop",
-  "Ticker.Reset",
-
-  # `asynctimerchan = godebug.New(...)`: `internal/godebug` is not translated
-  # (TrustedCode/time.lean)
-  "asynctimerchan",
-]
 translate = [
   "Time",
   "Duration",
@@ -89,3 +69,25 @@ translate = [
 
 # translate the definitions of all type declarations goose supports
 translate_types = true
+
+[[skip]]
+names = ["asynctimerchan"]
+reason = """
+`godebug.New("asynctimerchan")` (sleep.go): `internal/godebug` is not translated. Go's `New(name)` is
+`return &Setting{name: name}`, an allocation, so the initializer cannot panic and has no effect beyond
+the variable. Its only reader is `syncTimer`, which nothing in the model calls (`NewTimer`'s and
+`NewTicker`'s models choose their channel's kind themselves), so no translated code reads the
+unallocated variable.
+"""
+
+# The Go model (goose/model/overlay/time): each declaration's MODEL: comment says why it has at least
+# Go's behaviours. The runtime's bodyless clock readings (`runtimeNow`, `runtimeNano`,
+# `runtimeIsBubbled`, over the stub `now`) and timers (`newTimer`, `stopTimer`, `resetTimer`:
+# goroutines over the stubs of the runtime's timer state); `NewTimer`, `NewTicker` and `After` (the
+# runtime makes their channel synchronous, which `make` cannot express; `After` also waits on the
+# model's runtime clock, which `time.clockLb` speaks of); `Ticker.Stop` and `Ticker.Reset` (a
+# `*Ticker` to `*Timer` conversion through `unsafe.Pointer`).
+[model]
+dir = "goose/model/overlay/time"
+replaces = ["runtimeNow", "runtimeNano", "runtimeIsBubbled", "newTimer", "stopTimer", "resetTimer",
+  "NewTimer", "After", "NewTicker", "Ticker.Stop", "Ticker.Reset"]
```

### Perennial/Golang/Defn.lean  (tier 0, semantics)

-semantics guarantees. `pool_sem` is the Go model of the state of a `sync.Pool`

-(`TrustedCode/sync.lean`). -/
+semantics guarantees. -/

+export Semantics (sem_fn chan_sem string_sem)

### Perennial/Golang/Defn/Lock.lean  (tier 0, semantics)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/Golang/Defn/Lock.lean b/Perennial/Golang/Defn/Lock.lean
deleted file mode 100644
index 08e3b35d5..000000000
--- a/Perennial/Golang/Defn/Lock.lean
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,39 +0,0 @@
-/-
-A spin lock on a boolean location: `trylock`, `lock` and `unlock`.
--/
-module
-
-public import Perennial.Golang.Defn.Pre
-
-@[expose] public section
-
-namespace Perennial
-
-namespace lock
-section code
-variable [GooseExts] [GoGlobalContext]
-
-def trylock : val :=
-  λ: "m", Snd (CmpXchg "m" #false #true)
-
-set_option linter.iris.dupNamespace false in
-def lock : val :=
-  rec: "lock" "m" :=
-    if: Snd (CmpXchg "m" #false #true) then
-      #()
-    else
-      "lock" "m"
-
-/-- Unlocks the lock; stuck if it is not locked, where Go's `Mutex.Unlock` ends the program
-with `fatal("sync: unlock of unlocked mutex")` (`internal/sync/mutex.go`, `unlockSlow`),
-which `recover` cannot catch. -/
-def unlock : val :=
-  λ: "m", if: Snd (CmpXchg "m" #true #false) then #()
-    else Panic "sync: unlock of unlocked mutex"
-
-end code
-end lock
-
-attribute [irreducible] lock.trylock lock.lock lock.unlock
-
-end Perennial
```

### Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean  (tier 0, trusted-model)

```diff
diff --git a/Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean b/Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean
index 22f6739ea..bf2e46634 100644
--- a/Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean
+++ b/Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean
@@ -1,3583 +1,57 @@
 /-
-Trusted code for the Go `fmt` package, in namespace `fmt` (as the generated
-package).
-
-`Sprintf` and the printer it runs are goose's translation of the real `fmt` (`Perennial/Code/fmt.toml`,
-perennial#671 stage 1). Until stage 2 retires them, `Printf`, `Fprintf`, `Fprint` and `Errorf` are
-still the models here, each goose's translation of the Go its docstring quotes, with the edits it
-names; they do not use the real printer (nor its pool `ppFree`). They share `doPrintf`, which formats the common directives of
-arguments that Go formats without calling any of their methods (`inert`) exactly (`formatArg`), and
-the common directives of a `[]byte` or a `[]string` (`formatSlice`, reading its bytes or elements, as Go does),
-and from the first other directive on gives an arbitrary string while calling what Go calls of the
-arguments: their `Error()` or `String()` under the verbs `v s x X q` (`visitRest`, `visitArg`).
-`Errorf` then returns what Go's `errorf` returns: `errors.New`, a `*wrapError` or a `*wrapErrors`,
-by its `%w` directives. `Fprint` calls what Go calls of each operand under `%v` (`visitArg`) and
-writes an arbitrary string. Where the model cannot follow Go (a `Format` or `GoString` method Go would
-call, a `reflect.Value` under a method verb, which Go's `printArg` unwraps (the model tells it by its
-`CanInterface() bool` method), reflection into a struct,
-pointer, map or slice (other than a `[]byte` or a `[]string`), an argument index or a `*` width), it is stuck (`notModelled`). The model's Go is also in `goose/model/fmt/model_test.go`, which
-`goose/model/fmt/model_diff_test.go` runs against Go's `fmt`: same output (each arbitrary string
-standing for any string), same method calls in the same order, same error kind, on every call the
-model is not stuck on, and the model is not stuck on any call the specs admit (`fmt.argsOk`; `Fprint`'s, `fmt.printArgOk`).
--/

-
-public import Perennial.Golang.Defn.Pre
-public import Perennial.Code.errors
-public import Perennial.TrustedCode.arbitraryString
-public import Perennial.Code.io

-@[expose] public section
-
-set_option linter.iris.style.nameCheck false
-
-namespace Perennial

-namespace fmt
-section code

-after it (helpers of the model's `doPrintf`, like those below, not functions of package `fmt`).
-Goose's translation of
-
-```go
-func intVerb(format string, i int) (int, int, bool) {
-	if i+1 < len(format) && (format[i+1] == 'd' || format[i+1] == 'v') {
-		return 0, i + 2, true
-	}
-	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == '0' {

-/-- The integer an argument holds, as a sign and a magnitude, if its dynamic type is a type whose
-underlying type is one of Go's integer types (a named integer type, `type LeaseID int64`) and that
-implements none of `fmt.Formatter`, `fmt.Stringer` and `error`: Go's `printArg` then calls
-`handleMethods`, which finds no method to call, and `printValue` formats `reflect.ValueOf(x)`
-by its kind, as `fmtInteger` formats the underlying integer. (`fmt.GoStringer` is consulted
-only by `%#v`, which the model does not format.) Goose's translation (by the goose of this
-revision) of
-
-```go
-func namedIntArg(x any) (bool, uint64, bool) {
-	if _, ok := x.(interface{ Format(State, rune) }); ok {
-		return false, 0, false
-	}
-	if _, ok := x.(interface{ String() string }); ok {
-		return false, 0, false
-	}
-	if _, ok := x.(error); ok {
-		return false, 0, false
-	}
-	var n int64
-	if v, ok := underlyingAs[int](x); ok {
-		n = int64(v)
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int64](x); ok {
-		n = v
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int32](x); ok {
-		n = int64(v)
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int16](x); ok {
-		n = int64(v)
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int8](x); ok {
-		n = int64(v)
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint](x); ok {
-		return false, uint64(v), true
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint64](x); ok {
-		return false, v, true
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint32](x); ok {
-		return false, uint64(v), true
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint16](x); ok {
-		return false, uint64(v), true
-	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint8](x); ok {
-		return false, uint64(v), true
-	} else {
-		return false, 0, false
-	}
-	if n < 0 {
-		return true, uint64(-n), true
-	}
-	return false, uint64(n), true
-}
-```
-
-where `State` is `fmt.State` and `underlyingAs[T](x)` is a stub `func underlyingAs[T any](x any)
-(T, bool)`, with two edits: goose's call `FuncResolve underlyingAs [T] #()` is replaced by the
-instruction `TypeAssertUnderlying2 T` (`reflect.ValueOf(x)` read at `T`'s kind, which plain Go
-cannot express), and the scratch package's `State.ty` by `go.GoType.Named go!"fmt.State" []`
-(`fmt.State.ty`). -/

-/-- The lowercase hexadecimal of the integer of sign `neg` and magnitude `mag`, without a `0x`

-	if neg {
-		return "-" + digits

-	return digits
-}

-/-- The string an argument holds, if its dynamic type is `string` or a type whose underlying type
-is `string` (a named string type, `type UID string`) and that implements none of `fmt.Formatter`,
-`fmt.Stringer` and `error`: Go's `printArg` formats a `string` with `fmtString`, and a value of a
-named string type falls to `handleMethods`, which finds no method to call, and `printValue`
-formats `reflect.ValueOf(x)` by its kind, `String`, with `fmtString` of `Value.String()`, its
-string. (A `string` has no methods, so the checks below are false for it. `fmt.GoStringer` is
-consulted only by `%#v`, which the model does not format.) Goose's translation (by the goose of
-this revision) of
-
-```go
-func strArg(x any) (string, bool) {
-	if _, ok := x.(interface{ Format(State, rune) }); ok {
-		return "", false
-	}

-/-- At `format[i]`, a `%`: the directive's output for the argument `x` and the index after it, if
-the model formats it: `%s` or `%v` of a string (`strArg`: a `string`, or a named string type
-without formatting methods), `%x` of an integer (`anyIntArg`), or an integer directive (`intVerb`,
-which takes `%v` too) of an integer. Goose's translation (by the goose of this revision, with its function
-results in cells `$resI`) of
-
-```go
-func formatArg(format string, i int, x any) (string, int, bool) {
-	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == 's' {
-		s, ok := strArg(x)
-		return s, i + 2, ok
-	}
-	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == 'v' {
-		if s, ok := strArg(x); ok {
-			return s, i + 2, true
-		}
-	}
-	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == 'x' {
-		neg, mag, ok := anyIntArg(x)
-		if !ok {
-			return "", 0, false
-		}
-		return formatHex(neg, mag), i + 2, true
-	}
-	width, next, ok := intVerb(format, i)
-	if !ok {
-		return "", 0, false
-	}
-	neg, mag, ok2 := anyIntArg(x)
-	if !ok2 {
-		return "", 0, false
-	}
-	return formatInt(neg, mag, width), next, true
-}
-```
-
-with the calls running `fmt.strArg`, `fmt.intVerb`, `fmt.anyIntArg`, `fmt.formatInt` and
-`fmt.formatHex` above
-(goose's `FuncResolve f [] #()` replaced by `f`, the only edit). `%v` of a `string` is the string
-itself, as `%s` gives it: Go's `fmt` documents the default format `%v` of a string as `%s`, and
-`printArg` formats a `string` argument with `fmtString`, whose `'v'` case (without the `#` flag) is
-`fmtS`, the `'s'` case, which copies the bytes unchanged when there is no precision or width.
-`goose/model/fmt/sprintf_test.go` checks that `fmt.Sprintf` gives every string, byte for byte, for
-`%v` as for `%s` (the empty string, `%`, invalid UTF-8, NUL), and the same for a named string type
-without formatting methods; a named string type with a `String`, `Error` or `Format` method
-(whose call the model does not make) makes the rest of the output arbitrary; `doPrintf` calls
-`formatArg` only for an argument `inert` says has none of these methods. -/

-/-- The byte `c` as `strconv.Quote` writes it inside the quotes, for `c` below `0x80` (a helper of `quoteBytes`): `\"` and `\\`, the bytes from `0x20` to `0x7E` themselves, the escapes `\a \b \f \n \r \t \v`, and `\xNN` for the other control bytes and `0x7F` (there `strconv`'s escaping depends on no table). Goose's translation (by the goose of this revision, with its function results in cells `$resI`) of
-
-```go
-func quoteByte(c byte) string {
-	if c == '"' || c == '\\' {
-		return string([]byte{'\\', c})
-	}
-	if 0x20 <= c && c <= 0x7e {
-		return string([]byte{c})
-	}
-	if c == '\a' {
-		return `\a`
-	}
-	if c == '\b' {
-		return `\b`
-	}
-	if c == '\f' {
-		return `\f`
-	}
-	if c == '\n' {
-		return `\n`
-	}
-	if c == '\r' {
-		return `\r`
-	}
-	if c == '\t' {
-		return `\t`
-	}
-	if c == '\v' {
-		return `\v`
-	}
-	return string([]byte{'\\', 'x', hexNibble(c/16, false), hexNibble(c%16, false)})
-}
-```
