### goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go b/goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..59034e992
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/fmt/print.go
@@ -0,0 +1,152 @@
+// The Go models of fmt (Perennial/Code/fmt.toml, [model]): errorf, Fprintf, pp.doPrint and
+// pp.doPrintln, spliced into errors.go and print.go in place of the real declarations, printfFormat
+// (the formatting of Printf, which is trusted: Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean), and the additions
+// they need besides the model printer (printmodel.go). Sprintf, Errorf, Fprint, Fprintln and the
+// real printer stay Go's own, translated.
+//
+// Why models at all: the real print path (pp.doPrintf, pp.printArg) is translated and proved only
+// on stage 1's subset (fmt.Fits, perennial#671). Elsewhere it is stuck: on floats and complex
+// numbers (strconv.AppendFloat and pp.fmtComplex are not translated), on %q of an integer
+// (strconv.AppendQuoteRune), on %T, %p, a bad verb and %!(EXTRA ...) (reflect.TypeOf), and on
+// named integer and string types, a []string, and a value of integer kind with String or Error
+// under a non-method verb (pp.printValue, which reflects). The specs of Errorf, Printf, Fprintf
+// and Fprint admit those calls (fmt.argsOk, fmt.printArgOk), so these four print with the model
+// printer instead (Printf through printfFormat): doPrintf formats exactly the common directives of inert arguments and of a
+// []byte or []string, appending them to the printer's buffer p.buf as Go does, and from the first
+// other directive on appends arbitrary strings, before each call of an argument's Error() or
+// String() (where Go's handleMethods calls it) and at the end. The buffer is observable outside
+// fmt: Fprintf and Fprint hand p.buf to w.Write, and a writer that keeps the slice aliases the
+// buffer of a printer the pool (ppFree) hands out again, to a later errorf, Fprintf or Fprint, or
+// to a method that the same call calls; so the model appends to p.buf what Go has appended by each
+// method call (an arbitrary string covers it), and errorf reads its message back from p.buf. it is stuck (notModelled)
+// where it does not follow Go: a Format or GoString method Go would call, a reflect.Value,
+// reflection into a struct, pointer, map or other slice, an argument index or a '*' width with an
+// argument that is not inert, a method that panics (Go's catchPanic recovers it). Its output is
+// Go's, the arbitrary strings standing for any suffix, and it calls the methods Go calls in Go's
+// order: model ⊇ Go, checked by goose/model/fmt/model_diff_test.go (TestModelSprintf,
+// TestModelBytesExact, TestModelStringsExact, TestModelErrorf, TestModelFprintf, TestModelFprint,
+// TestModelFprintln, TestModelPrintf) and sprintf_test.go, and, for the buffer, by TestModelAlias
+// (testdata/alias, run by etc/model-tests.sh under the overlay).
+
+package fmt
+
+import (
+	"internal/gomodel"
+	"internal/stringslite"
+	"io"
+	"reflect"
+)
+
+// MODEL: errorf is Go's, but for its printer: Go's p.doPrintf(format, a), with p.wrapErrs set, is
+// the model printer's doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, true), which appends the message to p.buf (Go's,
+// with arbitrary strings from the first directive it does not format) and gives the indices of the
+// %w arguments (or that they are unknown); errorfResult builds the error from them as Go's switch
+// on len(p.wrappedErrs) does: errors.New, a *wrapError, a *wrapErrors (see the file comment for
+// what the model printer covers and where it is stuck; TestModelErrorf compares the error's kind,
+// message and wrapped errors with Go's). The printer is Go's own, from newPrinter and back to the
+// pool with p.free(), and the message is read back from p.buf as Go reads it: the pool is
+// observable outside fmt, since Fprintf hands p.buf to a writer, which may keep the slice and see
+// what a later errorf writes into the same printer's buffer, or write into it itself
+// (TestModelAlias, goose/model/fmt).
+func errorf(format string, a ...any) error {
+	if len(a) == 0 && stringslite.IndexByte(format, '%') == -1 {
+		return nil
+	}
+	p := newPrinter()
+	wrapped, unknown := doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, true)
+	s := string(p.buf)
+	err := errorfResult(s, wrapped, unknown, a)
+	p.free()
+	return err
+}
+
+// MODEL: Fprintf is Go's, but for one line: Go's p.doPrintf(format, a) is the model printer's
+// doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, false), which appends to p.buf what Go appends up to its first
+// method call or directive the model does not format, and arbitrary strings from there, before
+// each method call and at the end (see the file comment for what the model printer covers and
+// where it is stuck; TestModelFprintf checks the bytes Write receives and the methods called;
+// TestModelAlias a method reading p.buf through a slice an earlier Write kept). The printer, its
+// pool (ppFree), the one w.Write(p.buf) and p.free() are Go's own.
+func Fprintf(w io.Writer, format string, a ...any) (n int, err error) {
+	p := newPrinter()
+	doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, false)
+	n, err = w.Write(p.buf)
+	p.free()
+	return
+}
+
+// printfFormat is Printf's formatting, a helper of its trusted model (Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean,
+// fmt.toml's trusted), not a function of package fmt: Go's Printf is Fprintf(os.Stdout, format,
+// a...), whose printer, model doPrintf (as in Fprintf above) and p.free() this is, without the
+// write to os.Stdout; the trusted Printf returns an arbitrary count and either nil or an arbitrary
+// error for it (the model of os has no Stdout, and translating the write would need a semantics
+// primitive for an arbitrary interface value, Go's error of it).
+func printfFormat(format string, a []any) {
+	p := newPrinter()
+	doPrintf(&p.buf, format, a, false)
+	p.free()
+}
+
+// MODEL: pp.doPrint prints the operands under %v, a space between two that are not strings. Go
+// tells a string by reflect.TypeOf(arg).Kind() == reflect.String (reflection, which is stuck) and
+// prints each with p.printArg, which is stuck on the operands of the file comment; Fprint's spec
+// admits them (fmt.printArgOk). The model calls of each operand what Go's printArg calls under %v
+// (visitArg: its Error() or String(), stuck where the model printer is), after appending arbitrary
+// strings to p.buf (writeArbitrary), which stand for what Go has appended by then (a method may
+// read p.buf through a slice a writer kept), and appends arbitrary strings at the end, for the
+// rest of Go's output (TestModelFprint checks the calls, TestModelAlias the buffer). Fprint and
+// Sprint, Go's own, print with it.
+func (p *pp) doPrint(a []any) {
+	for _, x := range a {
+		writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
+		visitArg(x, 'v', false, false)
+	}
+	writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
+}
+
+// MODEL: pp.doPrintln is doPrint's model (see there): Go's doPrintln, for each operand, appends a
+// space if it is not the first and prints it with p.printArg(arg, 'v'), then appends '\n'; the
+// model calls of each operand what printArg calls under %v (visitArg), after arbitrary strings
+// (writeArbitrary), which cover Go's space and what printArg appended before, and ends with
+// arbitrary strings, which cover the rest and the newline (TestModelFprintln checks the calls).
+// Fprintln and Sprintln, Go's own, print with it.
+func (p *pp) doPrintln(a []any) {
+	for _, x := range a {
+		writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
+		visitArg(x, 'v', false, false)
+	}
+	writeArbitrary(&p.buf)
+}
+
+// chooseString is an arbitrary string, the model printer's output from where it stops following
+// Go: it can return every string (and may also not terminate, which partial correctness allows).
+func chooseString() string {
+	var s string
+	for gomodel.ChooseUint64() != 0 {
+		s += string([]byte{byte(gomodel.ChooseUint64())})
+	}
+	return s
+}
+
+// notModelled is the model printer's stuck point, where it does not follow Go: gomodel.Stuck.
+func notModelled() {
+	gomodel.Stuck()
+}
+
+// underlyingAs[T](x) is x's value converted to T and true, if x's dynamic type has T's underlying
+// type, and the zero T and false otherwise (also for a nil x): reflect.ValueOf(x).Kind() is T's
+// kind, which plain Go cannot test without reflection. A primitive stub (fmt.toml, trusted): its
+// GooseLang model is TypeAssertUnderlying2 T (Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean), and this body, for
+// gc builds, is the reflection, for the integer types and string that the model printer asks.
+func underlyingAs[T any](x any) (T, bool) {
+	var z T
+	if x == nil {
+		return z, false
+	}
+	v := reflect.ValueOf(x)
+	zt := reflect.TypeOf(z)
+	if v.Kind() != zt.Kind() {
+		return z, false
+	}
+	return v.Convert(zt).Interface().(T), true
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/fmt/printmodel.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/fmt/printmodel.go b/goose/model/overlay/fmt/printmodel.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..010b0cd6c
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/fmt/printmodel.go
@@ -0,0 +1,691 @@
+// The model printer of fmt (Perennial/Code/fmt.toml, [model]): additions that the models of
+// errorf, Fprintf, printfFormat (Printf's) and pp.doPrint (print.go of this directory) call in place of Go's
+// printer for the formatting the real print path is stuck on. Translated like the rest of fmt;
+// they carry no claim of their own: the claims are the MODEL: comments of the replaced
+// declarations, which call them. goose/model/fmt/model_test.go is this file rewritten for the
+// differential tests (goose/model/fmt/model_copy_test.go checks the rewrite).
+
+package fmt
+
+import (
+	"errors"
+	"internal/gomodel"
+)
+
+// kindIs[T](x): whether x's dynamic type has T's underlying type. A primitive stub (fmt.toml,
+// trusted), as underlyingAs is: its GooseLang model is the second result of TypeAssertUnderlying2 T
+// (Perennial/TrustedCode/fmt.lean), and this body, for gc builds, is underlyingAs's second result.
+// (It is not translated from this body: a translation binds underlyingAs's first result, the value
+// x holds, which the logic cannot step past for an arbitrary argument, as nothing says that value
+// is not a panic value; isIntKind and isStrKind ask kindIs of any argument.)
+func kindIs[T any](x any) bool {
+	_, ok := underlyingAs[T](x)
+	return ok
+}
+
+func intVerb(format string, i int) (int, int, bool) {
+	if i+1 < len(format) && (format[i+1] == 'd' || format[i+1] == 'v') {
+		return 0, i + 2, true
+	}
+	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == '0' {
+		j := i + 2
+		var width int
+		for j < len(format) && '0' <= format[j] && format[j] <= '9' && width <= 1000000 {
+			width = width*10 + int(format[j]-'0')
+			j++
+		}
+		if j > i+2 && j < len(format) && format[j] == 'd' && width <= 1000000 {
+			return width, j + 1, true
+		}
+	}
+	return 0, 0, false
+}
+
+func intArg(x any) (bool, uint64, bool) {
+	var n int64
+	if v, ok := x.(int); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := x.(int64); ok {
+		n = v
+	} else if v, ok := x.(int32); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := x.(int16); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := x.(int8); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := x.(uint); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else if v, ok := x.(uint64); ok {
+		return false, v, true
+	} else if v, ok := x.(uint32); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else if v, ok := x.(uint16); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else if v, ok := x.(uint8); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else {
+		return false, 0, false
+	}
+	if n < 0 {
+		return true, uint64(-n), true
+	}
+	return false, uint64(n), true
+}
+
+func namedIntArg(x any) (bool, uint64, bool) {
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ Format(State, rune) }); ok {
+		return false, 0, false
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ String() string }); ok {
+		return false, 0, false
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(error); ok {
+		return false, 0, false
+	}
+	var n int64
+	if v, ok := underlyingAs[int](x); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int64](x); ok {
+		n = v
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int32](x); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int16](x); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[int8](x); ok {
+		n = int64(v)
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint](x); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint64](x); ok {
+		return false, v, true
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint32](x); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint16](x); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else if v, ok := underlyingAs[uint8](x); ok {
+		return false, uint64(v), true
+	} else {
+		return false, 0, false
+	}
+	if n < 0 {
+		return true, uint64(-n), true
+	}
+	return false, uint64(n), true
+}
+
+func anyIntArg(x any) (bool, uint64, bool) {
+	neg, mag, ok := intArg(x)
+	if ok {
+		return neg, mag, true
+	}
+	return namedIntArg(x)
+}
+
+func formatInt(neg bool, mag uint64, width int) string {
+	var digits string
+	for {
+		digits = string([]byte{byte(mag%10) + '0'}) + digits
+		mag = mag / 10
+		if mag == 0 {
+			break
+		}
+	}
+	if neg {
+		width--
+	}
+	for len(digits) < width {
+		digits = "0" + digits
+	}
+	if neg {
+		return "-" + digits
+	}
+	return digits
+}
+
+func formatHex(neg bool, mag uint64) string {
+	var digits string
+	for {
+		d := byte(mag % 16)
+		if d < 10 {
+			d = '0' + d
+		} else {
+			d = 'a' + (d - 10)
+		}
+		digits = string([]byte{d}) + digits
+		mag = mag / 16
+		if mag == 0 {
+			break
+		}
+	}
+	if neg {
+		return "-" + digits
+	}
+	return digits
+}
+
+func strArg(x any) (string, bool) {
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ Format(State, rune) }); ok {
+		return "", false
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ String() string }); ok {
+		return "", false
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(error); ok {
+		return "", false
+	}
+	return underlyingAs[string](x)
+}
+
+func formatArg(format string, i int, x any) (string, int, bool) {
+	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == 's' {
+		s, ok := strArg(x)
+		return s, i + 2, ok
+	}
+	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == 'v' {
+		if s, ok := strArg(x); ok {
+			return s, i + 2, true
+		}
+	}
+	if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == 'x' {
+		neg, mag, ok := anyIntArg(x)
+		if !ok {
+			return "", 0, false
+		}
+		return formatHex(neg, mag), i + 2, true
+	}
+	width, next, ok := intVerb(format, i)
+	if !ok {
+		return "", 0, false
+	}
+	neg, mag, ok2 := anyIntArg(x)
+	if !ok2 {
+		return "", 0, false
+	}
+	return formatInt(neg, mag, width), next, true
+}
+
+// hexNibble: the hexadecimal digit of d (below 16), lowercase or uppercase.
+func hexNibble(d byte, upper bool) byte {
+	if d < 10 {
+		return '0' + d
+	}
+	if upper {
+		return 'A' + (d - 10)
+	}
+	return 'a' + (d - 10)
+}
+
+// quoteByte: the byte c as strconv.Quote writes it inside the quotes, for c below 0x80: \" and
+// \\, the bytes from 0x20 to 0x7E themselves, the escapes \a \b \f \n \r \t \v, and \xNN for the
+// other control bytes and 0x7F. (Of a byte from 0x80 it gives \xNN too; quoteBytes never asks.)
+func quoteByte(c byte) string {
+	if c == '"' || c == '\\' {
+		return string([]byte{'\\', c})
+	}
+	if 0x20 <= c && c <= 0x7e {
+		return string([]byte{c})
+	}
+	if c == '\a' {
+		return `\a`
+	}
+	if c == '\b' {
+		return `\b`
+	}
+	if c == '\f' {
+		return `\f`
+	}
+	if c == '\n' {
+		return `\n`
+	}
+	if c == '\r' {
+		return `\r`
+	}
+	if c == '\t' {
+		return `\t`
+	}
+	if c == '\v' {
+		return `\v`
+	}
+	return string([]byte{'\\', 'x', hexNibble(c/16, false), hexNibble(c%16, false)})
+}
+
+// quoteBytes: strconv.Quote(string(b)), as fmtQ prints %q of b without flags, width or
+// precision, when every byte of b is ASCII (below 0x80): there strconv's escaping depends on no
+// table (IsPrint is true exactly from 0x20 to 0x7E). ok is false when a byte is not ASCII, where
+// strconv decodes UTF-8 and consults its printable-rune tables.
+func quoteBytes(b []byte) (string, bool) {
+	out := "\""
+	for _, c := range b {
+		if c >= 0x80 {
+			return "", false
+		}
+		out += quoteByte(c)
+	}
+	return out + "\"", true
+}
+
+// decBytes: %v and %d of b, as fmtBytes prints them: its bytes in decimal, separated by spaces,
+// in brackets.
+func decBytes(b []byte) string {
+	out := "["
+	for k, c := range b {
+		if k > 0 {
+			out += " "
+		}
+		out += formatInt(false, uint64(c), 0)
+	}
+	return out + "]"
+}
+
+// hexBytes: %x (upper false) and %X of b, as fmtBytes prints them: two hexadecimal digits a byte.
+func hexBytes(b []byte, upper bool) string {
+	var out string
+	for _, c := range b {
+		out += string([]byte{hexNibble(c/16, upper), hexNibble(c%16, upper)})
+	}
+	return out
+}
+
+// joinStrings: %v and %s of ss, as printValue prints a []string: its elements (each by fmtS, the
+// string itself), separated by spaces, in brackets.
+func joinStrings(ss []string) string {
+	out := "["
+	for k, s := range ss {
+		if k > 0 {
+			out += " "
+		}
+		out += s
+	}
+	return out + "]"
+}
+
+// formatSlice: at format[i], a '%': the output of the directive for the argument x, if it is of
+// type []byte or []string, and the index after it. Of a []byte, as Go's fmtBytes prints it, if
+// the directive is %s, %v, %d, %x, %X or %q, without flags, width or precision (%q only of ASCII
+// bytes, quoteBytes). Of a []string, as printValue prints it (no method of a []string or a string
+// to call), if the directive is %s or %v without flags, width or precision (joinStrings). It reads
+// every byte or element of x then, as Go does, and nothing otherwise.
+func formatSlice(format string, i int, x any) (string, int, bool) {
+	if i+1 >= len(format) {
+		return "", 0, false
+	}
+	verb := format[i+1]
+	if verb != 's' && verb != 'v' && verb != 'd' && verb != 'x' && verb != 'X' && verb != 'q' {
+		return "", 0, false
+	}
+	if ss, ok := x.([]string); ok {
+		if verb == 's' || verb == 'v' {
+			return joinStrings(ss), i + 2, true
+		}
+		return "", 0, false
+	}
+	b, ok := x.([]byte)
+	if !ok {
+		return "", 0, false
+	}
+	if verb == 's' {
+		return string(b), i + 2, true
+	}
+	if verb == 'v' || verb == 'd' {
+		return decBytes(b), i + 2, true
+	}
+	if verb == 'x' || verb == 'X' {
+		return hexBytes(b, verb == 'X'), i + 2, true
+	}
+	if verb == 'q' {
+		s, ok := quoteBytes(b)
+		if !ok {
+			return "", 0, false
+		}
+		return s, i + 2, true
+	}
+	return "", 0, false
+}
+
+// isBasic: whether x's dynamic type is one of the predeclared types that printArg formats by its
+// type switch, without handleMethods (bool, the integer types, uintptr, float32, float64, string;
+// not complex64, complex128 or []byte, which the model does not follow, nor reflect.Value, which
+// printArg unwraps: visitArg is stuck on a Stringer with a CanInterface() bool method, as it has).
+func isBasic(x any) bool {
+	switch x.(type) {
+	case bool, int, int8, int16, int32, int64, uint, uint8, uint16, uint32, uint64, uintptr,
+		float32, float64, string:
+		return true
+	}
+	return false
+}
+
+// isIntKind: whether x's dynamic type has an integer kind (one of Go's ten integer types as its
+// underlying type; not uintptr).
+func isIntKind(x any) bool {
+	return kindIs[int](x) || kindIs[int64](x) || kindIs[int32](x) || kindIs[int16](x) ||
+		kindIs[int8](x) || kindIs[uint](x) || kindIs[uint64](x) || kindIs[uint32](x) ||
+		kindIs[uint16](x) || kindIs[uint8](x)
+}
+
+// isStrKind: whether x's dynamic type has kind String.
+func isStrKind(x any) bool {
+	return kindIs[string](x)
+}
+
+// hasFmtMethod: whether x implements one of the interfaces handleMethods consults (Formatter,
+// GoStringer, error, Stringer).
+func hasFmtMethod(x any) bool {
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ Format(State, rune) }); ok {
+		return true
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ GoString() string }); ok {
+		return true
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(error); ok {
+		return true
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ String() string }); ok {
+		return true
+	}
+	return false
+}
+
+// inert: whether Go formats x, under any directive, without calling any of its methods or reading
+// memory: nil, a predeclared basic type, or a type of integer or string kind that implements none
+// of handleMethods' interfaces.
+func inert(x any) bool {
+	if x == nil {
+		return true
+	}
+	if isBasic(x) {
+		return true
+	}
+	if hasFmtMethod(x) {
+		return false
+	}
+	if isStrKind(x) {
+		return true
+	}
+	return isIntKind(x)
+}
+
+// allInert: whether every argument is inert.
+func allInert(a []any) bool {
+	for _, x := range a {
+		if !inert(x) {
+			return false
+		}
+	}
+	return true
+}
+
+// parseDirective reads the directive whose bytes start at format[i], after its '%', as doPrintf
+// does: flags among '#', '0', '+', '-', ' '; a decimal width; '.' and a decimal precision (only if
+// a byte follows the '.'); the verb. It returns the verb (as an int; -1 if the format ends first,
+// Go's %!(NOVERB)), whether the directive is %#v or %#w (Go's sharpV), and the index after it.
+// ok is false where the model does not follow Go: a '*' width or precision, an argument index
+// '[', or a width or precision of more than 7 digits.
+func parseDirective(format string, i int) (int, bool, int, bool) {
+	var sharp bool
+	var phase int // 0: flags, 1: width, 2: precision
+	var n int     // digits read of the width or precision
+	for i < len(format) {
+		c := format[i]
+		if phase == 0 && c == '#' {
+			sharp = true
+			i++
+			continue
+		}
+		if phase == 0 && (c == '+' || c == '-' || c == '0' || c == ' ') {
+			i++
+			continue
+		}
+		if '0' <= c && c <= '9' {
+			if phase == 0 {
+				phase = 1
+				n = 1
+				i++
+				continue
+			}
+			if n >= 7 {
+				return 0, false, 0, false
+			}
+			n++
+			i++
+			continue
+		}
+		if phase < 2 && c == '.' && i+1 < len(format) {
+			phase = 2
+			n = 0
+			i++
+			continue
+		}
+		if c == '*' || c == '[' {
+			return 0, false, 0, false
+		}
+		return int(c), sharp && (c == 'v' || c == 'w'), i + 1, true
+	}
+	return -1, false, i, true
+}
+
+// visitKind: printValue of x by its kind, without its methods: the model follows it for the
+// integer and string kinds, which read no memory, and is not defined for the others.
+func visitKind(x any) {
+	if isStrKind(x) {
+		return
+	}
+	if isIntKind(x) {
+		return
+	}
+	notModelled()
+}
+
+// visitArg calls the methods of x that Go's printArg calls under the directive (verb, sharpV),
+// and nothing else; the output is not modelled.
+// callError calls e.Error() as printArg's handleMethods does. Go calls it under catchPanic, which
+// recovers a panic of the method and prints it ("%!v(PANIC=Error method: ...)"), then goes on; the
+// model does not follow that: it is stuck there (notModelled), never panicking where Go recovers.
+func callError(e error) {
+	defer func() {
+		if recover() != nil {
+			notModelled()
+		}
+	}()
+	e.Error()
+}
+
+// callString calls s.String(), as callError calls Error().
+func callString(s interface{ String() string }) {
+	defer func() {
+		if recover() != nil {
+			notModelled()
+		}
+	}()
+	s.String()
+}
+
+func visitArg(x any, verb byte, sharpV bool, wrapErrs bool) {
+	if x == nil {
+		return
+	}
+	if verb == 'T' || verb == 'p' {
+		return
+	}
+	if isBasic(x) {
+		return
+	}
+	if b, ok := x.([]byte); ok {
+		_ = string(b) // printArg's fmtBytes: reads b, calls no method, under every verb
+		return
+	}
+	if ss, ok := x.([]string); ok {
+		_ = joinStrings(ss) // printValue: reads the elements, calls no method, under every verb
+		return
+	}
+	if verb == 'w' {
+		if _, ok := x.(error); !ok || !wrapErrs {
+			visitKind(x)
+			return
+		}
+		verb = 'v'
+	}
+	if _, ok := x.(interface{ Format(State, rune) }); ok {
+		notModelled()
+	}
+	if sharpV {
+		if _, ok := x.(interface{ GoString() string }); ok {
+			notModelled()
+		}
+	} else if verb == 'v' || verb == 's' || verb == 'x' || verb == 'X' || verb == 'q' {
+		if e, ok := x.(error); ok {
+			callError(e)
+			return
+		}
+		if s, ok := x.(interface{ String() string }); ok {
+			if _, ok := x.(interface{ CanInterface() bool }); ok {
+				notModelled() // a reflect.Value, which printArg unwraps
+			}
+			callString(s)
+			return
+		}
+	}
+	visitKind(x)
+}
+
+// visitRest goes through the directives of format from index i, the arguments from argNum,
+// calling what Go calls of each argument (visitArg), and then of each argument left over (Go's
+// %!(EXTRA ...), under %v). Before each argument it appends arbitrary strings to b
+// (writeArbitrary): what Go's printer has appended to its buffer by then, which a method of the
+// argument can read through a slice of the buffer that a writer kept (Fprintf hands the buffer to
+// w.Write, and the pool hands the printer out again). It returns the indices of the arguments of
+// the %w directives, and whether it gave up at a directive the model does not follow, every
+// argument being inert.
+func visitRest(b *buffer, format string, i int, a []any, argNum int, wrapErrs bool) ([]int, bool) {
+	var wrapped []int
+	for i < len(format) {
+		if format[i] != '%' {
+			i++
+			continue
+		}
+		verb, sharpV, next, ok := parseDirective(format, i+1)
+		if !ok {
+			if allInert(a) {
+				return wrapped, true
+			}
+			notModelled()
+		}
+		i = next
+		if verb < 0 || verb == '%' {
+			continue
+		}
+		if argNum >= len(a) {
+			continue
+		}
+		if verb == 'w' {
+			wrapped = append(wrapped, argNum)
+		}
+		writeArbitrary(b)
+		visitArg(a[argNum], byte(verb), sharpV, wrapErrs)
+		argNum++
+	}
+	for argNum < len(a) {
+		writeArbitrary(b)
+		visitArg(a[argNum], 'v', false, wrapErrs)
+		argNum++
+	}
+	return wrapped, false
+}
+
+// writeArbitrary appends one or more arbitrary strings to b (chooseString), each with one
+// writeString: Go's printer appends to its buffer piece by piece, and as GooseLang's append chooses
+// a new capacity freely when it reallocates, these appends can leave b as Go's leaves its buffer,
+// in content, backing array and capacity.
+func writeArbitrary(b *buffer) {
+	b.writeString(chooseString())
+	for gomodel.Choose() {
+		b.writeString(chooseString())
+	}
+}
+
+// doPrintf appends the formatted string to b and returns the indices of the arguments of the %w
+// directives, and whether those are unknown (visitRest gave up). It formats as the trusted model of
+// Sprintf did before stage 1 of perennial#671, up to the first directive it does not format or
+// whose argument is not inert, and appends that prefix to b, which Go's printer has appended by
+// then, before any method call; from there visitRest makes the calls Go makes, with arbitrary
+// strings appended before each, and arbitrary strings end the output.
+func doPrintf(b *buffer, format string, a []any, wrapErrs bool) ([]int, bool) {
+	var out string
+	var argNum int
+	var i int
+	for i < len(format) {
+		if format[i] != '%' {
+			out += string([]byte{format[i]})
+			i++
+			continue
+		}
+		if i+1 < len(format) && format[i+1] == '%' {
+			out += "%"
+			i += 2
+			continue
+		}
+		if argNum < len(a) {
+			s, next, ok := formatSlice(format, i, a[argNum])
+			if ok {
+				out += s
+				argNum++
+				i = next
+				continue
+			}
+		}
+		if argNum < len(a) && inert(a[argNum]) {
+			s, next, ok := formatArg(format, i, a[argNum])
+			if ok {
+				out += s
+				argNum++
+				i = next
+				continue
+			}
+		}
+		b.writeString(out)
+		wrapped, unknown := visitRest(b, format, i, a, argNum, wrapErrs)
+		writeArbitrary(b)
+		return wrapped, unknown
+	}
+	b.writeString(out)
+	if argNum < len(a) {
+		wrapped, unknown := visitRest(b, format, i, a, argNum, wrapErrs)
+		writeArbitrary(b)
+		return wrapped, unknown
+	}
+	return nil, false
+}
+
+// errorfResult: the error errorf returns of the message s, the indices wrapped of the arguments
+// of its %w directives, and whether those are unknown (doPrintf's results): errors.New(s) when no
+// %w directive had an argument, a *wrapError holding the argument as an error (nil if it is not
+// one) when one had, a *wrapErrors holding those of the arguments that are errors when several
+// had (Go sorts and deduplicates them only after an argument index, which the model does not
+// follow: parseDirective refuses '['). Where doPrintf gave up on a directive it does not follow,
+// every argument being inert (none is an error), the error is any of the three, holding no error.
+func errorfResult(s string, wrapped []int, unknown bool, a []any) error {
+	if unknown {
+		n := gomodel.ChooseUint64()
+		if n == 0 {
+			return errors.New(s)
+		}
+		if n == 1 {
+			return &wrapError{msg: s}
+		}
+		return &wrapErrors{msg: s}
+	}
+	if len(wrapped) == 0 {
+		return errors.New(s)
+	}
+	if len(wrapped) == 1 {
+		w := &wrapError{msg: s}
+		w.err, _ = a[wrapped[0]].(error)
+		return w
+	}
+	var errs []error
+	for _, argNum := range wrapped {
+		if e, ok := a[argNum].(error); ok {
+			errs = append(errs, e)
+		}
+	}
+	return &wrapErrors{s, errs}
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/bytealg.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/bytealg.go b/goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/bytealg.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..8125d4d1a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/bytealg.go
@@ -0,0 +1,39 @@
+// The Go model of bytealg.MakeNoZero (Perennial/Code/internal/bytealg.toml,
+// [model]), spliced into bytealg.go in place of the real declaration.
+
+package bytealg
+
+import "internal/gomodel"
+
+// MODEL: MakeNoZero(n) is a slice of length n and capacity at least n whose
+// bytes, up to the capacity, are not zeroed. Go's has no Go body (goose:
+// "external function"): the runtime defines it (runtime/slice.go,
+// `//go:linkname bytealg_MakeNoZero internal/bytealg.MakeNoZero`, which the
+// overlay blanks so that gc links this body instead) as
+//
+//	if uintptr(len) > maxAlloc {
+//		panicmakeslicelen()
+//	}
+//	cap := roundupsize(uintptr(len), true)
+//	return unsafe.Slice((*byte)(mallocgc(cap, nil, false)), cap)[:len]
+//
+// The model chooses the capacity (gomodel.ChooseUint64: any c >= n, which
+// includes the size class roundupsize rounds n up to) and each byte up to the
+// capacity (gomodel.ChooseUint64: mallocgc with needzero false returns memory
+// with whatever it held, which a Go program cannot predict). A negative n
+// panics in make with Go's message ("makeslice: len out of range"). An n
+// above maxAlloc (2^48), where gc panics, is not checked: the model
+// allocates, as the semantics' make and append do (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md,
+// "No limit on allocation size", which names MakeNoZero).
+func MakeNoZero(n int) []byte {
+	c := int(gomodel.ChooseUint64() >> 1)
+	if c < n {
+		c = n
+	}
+	b := make([]byte, n, c)
+	full := b[:cap(b)]
+	for i := range full {
+		full[i] = byte(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
+	}
+	return b
+}
```

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/init.go b/goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/init.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..219cd33e1
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/internal/bytealg/init.go
@@ -0,0 +1,31 @@
+// The Go model of internal/bytealg's init function (index_amd64.go's, the
+// package's only one; Perennial/Code/internal/bytealg.toml, [model]), spliced
+// into index_amd64.go in place of the real one.
+
+package bytealg
+
+import "internal/gomodel"
+
+// MODEL: the package's init sets MaxLen to 63 or to 31. Go's
+//
+//	if cpu.X86.HasAVX2 {
+//		MaxLen = 63
+//	} else {
+//		MaxLen = 31
+//	}
+//
+// reads internal/cpu.X86, which no Go package initializer sets: the runtime
+// fills it (cpu.Initialize, from schedinit) from the processor's cpuid
+// (assembly) before any package is initialized. Goose translates the real
+// init, but X86 would then stay zero and MaxLen always 31, more defined than
+// gc. The model chooses (gomodel.Choose: the processor's AVX2 bit, which the
+// semantics does not model) either value.
+//
+//gomodel:replace init.0 "if cpu.X86.HasAVX2 {"
+func init() {
+	if gomodel.Choose() {
+		MaxLen = 63
+	} else {
+		MaxLen = 31
+	}
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/internal/gomodel/choose.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/internal/gomodel/choose.go b/goose/model/overlay/internal/gomodel/choose.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..ac536f896
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/internal/gomodel/choose.go
@@ -0,0 +1,30 @@
+package gomodel
+
+// ChooseString is an arbitrary string: every string is a result (and the loop
+// may also not terminate). It is translated Go over Choose and ChooseUint64,
+// not a primitive: a model uses it where Go's value is outside what the model
+// tracks (a linker-set string, a symbol name, a command-line argument). In Go
+// it is "".
+func ChooseString() string {
+	var b []byte
+	for Choose() {
+		b = append(b, byte(ChooseUint64()))
+	}
+	return string(b)
+}
+
+// ChooseStrings is an arbitrary []string (nil or not, of any length, every
+// element any string; in particular both nil and a non-nil empty slice: Go's
+// os.runtime_args and syscall.runtime_envs return append([]string{}, ...),
+// never nil). It is translated Go over Choose and ChooseString. In Go it is
+// nil.
+func ChooseStrings() []string {
+	var a []string
+	if Choose() {
+		a = []string{}
+	}
+	for Choose() {
+		a = append(a, ChooseString())
+	}
+	return a
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go b/goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..430bdc0eb
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/internal/sync/mutex.go
@@ -0,0 +1,66 @@
+// The Go model of internal/sync's Mutex methods and of fatal
+// (Perennial/Code/internal/sync.toml, [model]), spliced into mutex.go and
+// runtime.go in place of the real declarations. The type Mutex is the real
+// one, translated.
+
+package sync
+
+import (
+	"internal/gomodel"
+	"sync/atomic"
+)
+
+// MODEL: Go's Lock is a compare-and-swap of state from 0 to mutexLocked, and
+// on failure lockSlow, which spins and parks on the runtime's semaphore
+// (runtime_SemacquireMutex, runtime_canSpin, runtime_doSpin,
+// runtime_nanotime, all without a Go body) and records waiters, wakeups and
+// starvation in state, read with plain loads that race with the
+// compare-and-swaps. Goose translates lockSlow, but the hooks have no body and
+// the racy reads are outside what the sequentially consistent semantics
+// models; and the protocol in state is shared with TryLock and Unlock, so
+// they are replaced together. The model keeps state at 0 (unlocked) or
+// mutexLocked and spins on the fast path's compare-and-swap until it
+// succeeds: Lock returns exactly when it takes the mutex, as in Go; a
+// spinning model goroutine where Go's parks is a schedule with the same
+// results (the model has no fairness and no starvation mode, which only
+// order waiters). A goroutine that never gets the mutex spins forever where
+// Go's parks forever: spinning for parking is partial correctness, and Go's
+// deadlock detector (a global deadlock becomes the fatal throw "all
+// goroutines are asleep") is not modelled (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md).
+func (m *Mutex) Lock() {
+	for !atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, 0, mutexLocked) {
+	}
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's TryLock returns false if state is locked or starving, or if its
+// compare-and-swap of state to locked loses to a concurrent change, else
+// true. The model's state has no starvation bit and no waiters, so where Go
+// fails on an unlocked mutex (starvation mode, or a waiter changing state),
+// the model fails by an arbitrary choice (gomodel.Choose); otherwise it is the
+// compare-and-swap of 0 to mutexLocked. It thus returns false in every case
+// Go does, and true only when it took the mutex, as in Go.
+func (m *Mutex) TryLock() bool {
+	if gomodel.Choose() {
+		return false
+	}
+	return atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, 0, mutexLocked)
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's Unlock subtracts mutexLocked from state and, if waiters remain,
+// wakes one (unlockSlow, through runtime_Semrelease); on an unlocked mutex it
+// calls fatal("sync: unlock of unlocked mutex"). The model's state has no
+// waiters (a spinning Lock sees the 0), so Unlock is the compare-and-swap of
+// mutexLocked to 0, and fatal where it fails, that is where the mutex is not
+// locked.
+func (m *Mutex) Unlock() {
+	if !atomic.CompareAndSwapInt32(&m.state, mutexLocked, 0) {
+		fatal("sync: unlock of unlocked mutex")
+	}
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's fatal (a runtime push linkname, no Go body) prints the message
+// and ends the program, and recover cannot catch it. The model has no
+// behaviour there (gomodel.Stuck): a verified program never calls it.
+func fatal(string) {
+	gomodel.Stuck()
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/log/log.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/log/log.go b/goose/model/overlay/log/log.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..7fcf5f23b
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/log/log.go
@@ -0,0 +1,69 @@
+// The Go models of log.Printf, log.Println and log's init function
+// (Perennial/Code/log.toml, [model]), spliced into log.go in place of the real
+// declarations; modelDiscard is an addition.
+
+package log
+
+import (
+	"fmt"
+	"internal/gomodel"
+)
+
+// MODEL: Printf is std.output(0, 2, func(b []byte) []byte { return
+// fmt.Appendf(b, format, v...) }). The standard logger std is not translated
+// (its initializer is New(os.Stderr, "", LstdFlags), and os.Stderr is not a
+// File in the model: perennial#663), nor is any function that changes it
+// (SetOutput, SetFlags, SetPrefix, Default, Logger's setters), so in every
+// execution of the model std is as New made it: flags LstdFlags, no prefix,
+// output os.Stderr. Its output method then:
+//   - returns at once if the output is io.Discard (l.isDiscard), which needs
+//     a SetOutput no execution reaches; the model has both outcomes anyway
+//     (gomodel.Choose: it formats or does nothing);
+//   - reads the clock (time.Now), the prefix and the flags (atomic loads), and
+//     with LstdFlags not the caller's file and line: no effect;
+//   - takes a buffer from bufferPool, formats the date and time into it, and
+//     calls the closure, which formats the arguments as fmt.Appendf does:
+//     fmt's printer and doPrintf, which call the arguments' Error, String and
+//     Format methods, the only effects on the program. The model formats them
+//     with fmt.Fprintf, which runs the same newPrinter, doPrintf and free;
+//   - appends a newline, takes std.outMu (held only by output, never while
+//     the arguments' methods run) and writes the bytes to standard error,
+//     ignoring the result: an effect outside the program, which the model
+//     drops, as it drops Fprintf's bytes (modelDiscard). The write can also
+//     block (a full stderr pipe, holding std.outMu) or end the process
+//     (EPIPE on fd 2: os.File.Write's epipecheck raises SIGPIPE): both stop
+//     the program there, so their traces are prefixes of the model's, which
+//     returns (partial correctness, as for an exit).
+//
+// Neither can panic other than through the arguments' methods.
+func Printf(format string, v ...any) {
+	if gomodel.Choose() {
+		fmt.Fprintf(modelDiscard{}, format, v...)
+	}
+}
+
+// MODEL: Println is Printf's model (see there) with fmt.Appendln(b, v...) in
+// place of Appendf: the model formats the arguments with fmt.Fprintln, which
+// runs the same newPrinter, doPrintln and free.
+func Println(v ...any) {
+	if gomodel.Choose() {
+		fmt.Fprintln(modelDiscard{}, v...)
+	}
+}
+
+// modelDiscard is the writer of the models above: Write accepts every byte
+// and does nothing, as io.Discard's.
+type modelDiscard struct{}
+
+func (modelDiscard) Write(p []byte) (int, error) {
+	return len(p), nil
+}
+
+// MODEL: log's init function stores a closure into
+// log/internal.DefaultOutput, the output of log/slog's default handler; it
+// cannot panic. log/slog and log/internal are not translated, so no execution
+// of the model reads that variable, and the model does nothing.
+//
+//gomodel:replace init.0 "internal.DefaultOutput = func(pc uintptr, data []byte) error {"
+func init() {
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/runtime/symtab.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/runtime/symtab.go b/goose/model/overlay/runtime/symtab.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..1bcba1406
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/runtime/symtab.go
@@ -0,0 +1,45 @@
+// The Go model of runtime.Frames.Next (Perennial/Code/runtime.toml, [model]),
+// spliced into symtab.go in place of the real declaration.
+
+package runtime
+
+import "internal/gomodel"
+
+// MODEL: ci.Next() is the next frame of the program counters ci.callers,
+// symbolized through the binary's tables (findfunc, the inlining tree, the
+// cgo symbolizer; goose: "inc or dec statement with unsupported type
+// uintptr", symtab.go:131), and whether another one follows. Go reads and
+// writes *ci, so a nil ci panics (nil dereference) and a concurrent call on
+// the same Frames races; the model's load and store of ci.callers do both.
+// The model stores ci.callers on every call, also on an exhausted Frames,
+// where Go only reads *ci: two concurrent Next calls on an exhausted Frames
+// race in the model and not in Go (extra behaviour, the safe direction: a
+// race is stuck in GooseLang, so a proof must exclude it). The model leaves *ci unchanged, where Go advances it, which no caller
+// observes (the fields are unexported). It does not read the words
+// ci.callers points to, which Go does (ci.callers[0]): a concurrent write to
+// them races in Go and not in the model, but Go reads them only to look them
+// up (any value gives some frames, a garbage one is skipped), and the model's
+// frames are arbitrary anyway, so no outcome of that race is missing.
+// Every exported field of the frame and more is arbitrary, a superset of
+// what Go returns (goose/model/runtime/callers_test.go: a nil Func for a frame
+// of a function inlined into its caller, Function/File/Line of the call
+// sites, the zero frame with more false once the frames are exhausted). A
+// non-nil Func is new(Func): Func is zero-size, and the semantics allocates a
+// zero-size value at an arbitrary non-null address that owns no memory, so
+// two results can be equal or not and a proof can read nothing through it, as
+// with Go's pointers into the binary's tables. startLine and Entry are
+// arbitrary too; funcInfo stays zero (unexported, read only by the runtime).
+func (ci *Frames) Next() (frame Frame, more bool) {
+	ci.callers = ci.callers
+	frame.PC = uintptr(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
+	if gomodel.Choose() {
+		frame.Func = new(Func)
+	}
+	frame.Function = gomodel.ChooseString()
+	frame.File = gomodel.ChooseString()
+	frame.Line = int(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
+	frame.startLine = int(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
+	frame.Entry = uintptr(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
+	more = gomodel.Choose()
+	return
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/runtime/traceback.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/runtime/traceback.go b/goose/model/overlay/runtime/traceback.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..4fa05f030
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/runtime/traceback.go
@@ -0,0 +1,37 @@
+// The model of runtime.callers (Perennial/Code/runtime.toml), added to
+// traceback.go: callers itself stays (gc runs the real stack walk, which
+// testing.callerName and the like need), and is trusted, its GooseLang model
+// (Perennial/TrustedCode/runtime.lean, callers.impl) a call of callersModel.
+
+package runtime
+
+import "internal/gomodel"
+
+// MODEL: callersModel(skip, pcbuf) is the model of callers(skip, pcbuf), which
+// Callers calls: Go walks the calling
+// goroutine's stack (getg, sys.GetCallerPC/GetCallerSP, compiler intrinsics
+// with no Go body, and the unwinder over the binary's tables) and writes the
+// return program counters of the frames after the first skip into pcbuf[0],
+// pcbuf[1], ..., stopping at the end of the stack or of pcbuf, and returns how
+// many it wrote (tracebackPCs writes pcBuf[n] only for n < len(pcBuf)). It
+// cannot panic: a skip past the stack's depth, or negative, writes fewer
+// entries or the whole buffer. The model writes some prefix of pcbuf with
+// arbitrary words and returns its length, every n from 0 to
+// min(len(pcbuf), 2^40-1). The bound is the model assumption that a stack has
+// fewer than 2^39 frames (README.md, "Model assumption (stack depth)";
+// Perennial/TrustedCode/runtime.lean, callers.impl), so that a caller growing
+// its buffer until it is not filled (zap's stacktrace.Capture) stops. (Go: n
+// < len(pcbuf) when the stack is shallower, 0 when skip is past it,
+// len(pcbuf) when the buffer is too short: goose/model/runtime/callers_test.go).
+// It writes the cells one at a time, as Go does, so a concurrent access to
+// pcbuf races in the model as in Go. (Not a replacement of callers: under gc,
+// gomodel.Choose is false, so this body writes nothing, and the testing
+// package panics when callers finds no frame.)
+func callersModel(skip int, pcbuf []uintptr) int {
+	n := 0
+	for n < len(pcbuf) && n < 1<<40-1 && gomodel.Choose() {
+		pcbuf[n] = uintptr(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
+		n++
+	}
+	return n
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/sync/pool.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/sync/pool.go b/goose/model/overlay/sync/pool.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..1429639cc
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/sync/pool.go
@@ -0,0 +1,107 @@
+// The Go model of sync.Pool's Get and Put (Perennial/Code/sync.toml,
+// [model]), spliced into pool.go in place of the real declarations, with its
+// helpers added. The type Pool is the real one, translated; the model keeps
+// its state in Pool's field local (an unsafe.Pointer, where Go keeps its
+// per-P pools).
+
+package sync
+
+import (
+	"internal/gomodel"
+	"sync/atomic"
+	"unsafe"
+)
+
+// poolModel is the model's state of a Pool: a multiset of the values Put and
+// not yet taken by a Get, under a lock. It is more nondeterministic than gc's
+// per-P pools, their victim cache and the clearing of both at each GC, so that
+// it covers them: Put may drop its value, and Get may ignore the pool even
+// when it is not empty; otherwise Get removes and returns an arbitrary
+// element. Each value Put is returned by at most one Get, as in gc, and the
+// lock makes a Put of x synchronize before the Get returning x, as the Pool
+// documentation promises.
+type poolModel struct {
+	mu    Mutex
+	items []any
+}
+
+// poolModelOf returns the state of the pool p, installing a new, empty one on
+// first use: p.local is nil in a zero Pool, and only this function writes it,
+// with a compare-and-swap from nil, so that racing first calls of Get and Put
+// agree on one state.
+func poolModelOf(p *Pool) *poolModel {
+	for {
+		if l := atomic.LoadPointer(&p.local); l != nil {
+			return poolModelAt(l)
+		}
+		atomic.CompareAndSwapPointer(&p.local, nil, unsafe.Pointer(new(poolModel)))
+	}
+}
+
+// poolModelAt is (*poolModel)(l): a primitive stub (GooseLang: the identity on
+// the address, Perennial/TrustedCode/sync.lean), since the semantics converts
+// an unsafe.Pointer only to a pointer to a plain type. poolModelOf calls it
+// only on a non-nil value of a Pool's field local, which is unexported and
+// which, with Get and Put modelled, only poolModelOf writes, always with a
+// *poolModel: so l is a *poolModel converted to unsafe.Pointer, and the
+// conversion back is the identity, in Go as in the model.
+func poolModelAt(l unsafe.Pointer) *poolModel {
+	return (*poolModel)(l)
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's Put ignores nil and otherwise pins the goroutine to its P and
+// stores x in that P's private slot or pushes it onto the P's lock-free
+// shared chain; the GC later moves the per-P pools to the victim cache and
+// then drops them. Goose translates it, but the translation is stuck at
+// indexLocal's conversion of a uintptr back to an unsafe.Pointer (pointer
+// arithmetic) and at poolDequeue's store of an any through an
+// unsafe.Pointer to its eface; runtime_procPin, which keeps the goroutine on
+// its P, has no Go body. The model ignores nil as Go does, and otherwise
+// adds x to the pool's state or drops it (gomodel.Choose: the GC may drop it
+// in Go before any Get).
+func (p *Pool) Put(x any) {
+	if x == nil {
+		return
+	}
+	s := poolModelOf(p)
+	if gomodel.Choose() {
+		return
+	}
+	s.mu.Lock()
+	s.items = append(s.items, x)
+	s.mu.Unlock()
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's Get pins the goroutine to its P and takes the P's private
+// value, else pops its shared chain, else steals from other Ps and the
+// victim cache (getSlow); if it found nothing and New is not nil, it returns
+// New(). Goose translates it but is stuck as for Put. The model's search
+// takes an arbitrary element of the pool's state or nothing, even when the
+// state is not empty (gomodel.Choose: Go's search may miss values in other
+// Ps' chains or dropped by the GC, and gomodel.ChooseUint64: which value it
+// finds); the rest, calling New, is Go's.
+func (p *Pool) Get() any {
+	s := poolModelOf(p)
+	var x any
+	s.mu.Lock()
+	n := uint64(len(s.items))
+	if n != 0 && !gomodel.Choose() {
+		i := gomodel.ChooseUint64() % n
+		x = s.items[i]
+		s.items[i] = s.items[n-1]
+		s.items = s.items[:n-1]
+	}
+	s.mu.Unlock()
+	if x == nil && p.New != nil {
+		x = p.New()
+	}
+	return x
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_registerPoolCleanup (a runtime push linkname, no Go
+// body) records poolCleanup for the GC to call at the start of each cycle;
+// poolCleanup moves the per-P pools to the victim cache. The model has no GC,
+// and its pools' state is not in the per-P pools, which only the replaced
+// Get and Put used, so recording it changes nothing.
+func runtime_registerPoolCleanup(cleanup func()) {
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/sync/runtime.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/sync/runtime.go b/goose/model/overlay/sync/runtime.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..bdacc64f4
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/sync/runtime.go
@@ -0,0 +1,141 @@
+// The Go model of sync's runtime hooks (Perennial/Code/sync.toml, [model]),
+// spliced into runtime.go in place of the real declarations. Each real one is
+// a function with no Go body that the runtime defines by a push linkname
+// (runtime/sema.go); the overlay blanks those directives, so that gc links
+// these bodies instead.
+
+package sync
+
+import (
+	"sync/atomic"
+)
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_Semacquire (runtime/sema.go, semacquire1) waits until
+// *s > 0 and decrements it, trying first
+//
+//	func cansemacquire(addr *uint32) bool {
+//		for {
+//			v := atomic.Load(addr)
+//			if v == 0 {
+//				return false
+//			}
+//			if atomic.Cas(addr, v, v-1) {
+//				return true
+//			}
+//		}
+//	}
+//
+// and, while that fails, parking on a queue that runtime_Semrelease wakes. The
+// model is cansemacquire's loop, retried until it succeeds: it returns exactly
+// when it has decremented a positive *s, as Go's does; a model goroutine
+// spinning where Go's parks is a schedule with the same results (the queue
+// only orders the waiters). A goroutine that never acquires spins forever
+// where Go's parks forever: spinning for parking is partial correctness, and
+// Go's deadlock detector (a global deadlock becomes the fatal throw "all
+// goroutines are asleep") is not modelled (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md).
+func runtime_Semacquire(s *uint32) {
+	for {
+		v := atomic.LoadUint32(s)
+		if v == 0 {
+			continue
+		}
+		if atomic.CompareAndSwapUint32(s, v, v-1) {
+			return
+		}
+	}
+}
+
+// MODEL: as runtime_Semacquire: Go's differs only in the reason it reports
+// for a parked goroutine and in synctest's durable blocking, which the model
+// does not observe.
+func runtime_SemacquireWaitGroup(s *uint32, synctestDurable bool) {
+	runtime_Semacquire(s)
+}
+
+// MODEL: as runtime_Semacquire: lifo and skipframes only order the waiters
+// and select the profiled stack frames.
+func runtime_SemacquireRWMutexR(s *uint32, lifo bool, skipframes int) {
+	runtime_Semacquire(s)
+}
+
+// MODEL: as runtime_Semacquire (see runtime_SemacquireRWMutexR).
+func runtime_SemacquireRWMutex(s *uint32, lifo bool, skipframes int) {
+	runtime_Semacquire(s)
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_Semrelease (semrelease1) atomically increments *s and
+// wakes a waiter if there is one (handing it the count directly if handoff);
+// the model's waiters spin, so it is the increment.
+func runtime_Semrelease(s *uint32, handoff bool, skipframes int) {
+	atomic.AddUint32(s, 1)
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_notifyListAdd (runtime/sema.go, notifyListAdd) is
+//
+//	return l.wait.Add(1) - 1
+//
+// the next ticket of the list, taken atomically; the model is the same over
+// sync/atomic (wait is the runtime's atomic.Uint32, a uint32 in sync's copy of
+// the type).
+func runtime_notifyListAdd(l *notifyList) uint32 {
+	return atomic.AddUint32(&l.wait, 1) - 1
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_notifyListWait (notifyListWait) returns at once if
+// ticket t has been notified, less(t, l.notify) with
+//
+//	func less(a, b uint32) bool {
+//		return int32(a-b) < 0
+//	}
+//
+// and otherwise parks until a notify call readies it, which the notify calls
+// do exactly when they advance l.notify past t (NotifyOne readies the waiter
+// whose ticket is the old l.notify, NotifyAll every waiter). The model spins
+// on the same test, reading l.notify atomically: it returns exactly when Go's
+// can. A goroutine that is never notified spins forever where Go's parks
+// forever: spinning for parking is partial correctness, and Go's deadlock
+// detector (a global deadlock becomes the fatal throw "all goroutines are
+// asleep") is not modelled (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md).
+func runtime_notifyListWait(l *notifyList, t uint32) {
+	for int32(t-atomic.LoadUint32(&l.notify)) >= 0 {
+	}
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_notifyListNotifyAll (notifyListNotifyAll) returns if
+// l.wait == l.notify (no waiter), and otherwise, under the list's lock, sets
+// l.notify to l.wait and readies every parked waiter. The model reads both and
+// sets l.notify with a compare-and-swap, retried if l.notify changed in
+// between: the store linearizes at the compare-and-swap, as Go's does under
+// the lock (the lock only orders the notify calls, which the compare-and-swap
+// also does). The waiters are readied by the new l.notify (see
+// runtime_notifyListWait).
+func runtime_notifyListNotifyAll(l *notifyList) {
+	for {
+		n := atomic.LoadUint32(&l.notify)
+		w := atomic.LoadUint32(&l.wait)
+		if n == w || atomic.CompareAndSwapUint32(&l.notify, n, w) {
+			return
+		}
+	}
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_notifyListNotifyOne (notifyListNotifyOne) returns if
+// l.wait == l.notify (no waiter), and otherwise, under the list's lock,
+// increments l.notify and readies the waiter holding the old value as its
+// ticket, if it has parked yet (if not, its Wait sees the ticket notified and
+// returns at once). The model is the same increment by compare-and-swap,
+// retried if l.notify changed in between (as for NotifyAll).
+func runtime_notifyListNotifyOne(l *notifyList) {
+	for {
+		n := atomic.LoadUint32(&l.notify)
+		if n == atomic.LoadUint32(&l.wait) || atomic.CompareAndSwapUint32(&l.notify, n, n+1) {
+			return
+		}
+	}
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's runtime_notifyListCheck throws if size is not the runtime's
+// size of notifyList; the type is the real one, so its size is the runtime's,
+// and the check passes.
+func runtime_notifyListCheck(size uintptr) {
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/syscall/prlimit.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/syscall/prlimit.go b/goose/model/overlay/syscall/prlimit.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..a0ab2f307
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/syscall/prlimit.go
@@ -0,0 +1,34 @@
+// The Go model of syscall.prlimit1 (Perennial/Code/syscall.toml, [model]),
+// spliced into zsyscall_linux_amd64.go (amd64 linux, the platform
+// Perennial translates) in place of the real declaration.
+
+package syscall
+
+import "internal/gomodel"
+
+// MODEL: prlimit1 is the prlimit64 system call: Go's body is
+// RawSyscall6(SYS_PRLIMIT64, uintptr(pid), uintptr(resource),
+// uintptr(unsafe.Pointer(newlimit)), uintptr(unsafe.Pointer(old)), 0, 0) and,
+// for a non-zero errno, errnoErr(e1). The conversions of pointers to uintptr
+// and the system call (assembly) have no semantics. The kernel reads
+// *newlimit if newlimit is non-nil, writes *old if old is non-nil and the call
+// succeeds, and returns 0 or an errno; it cannot fault on Go's pointers and
+// cannot panic. The model reads *newlimit (so a concurrent write races, as
+// with the kernel's read), may write arbitrary limits to *old (a superset of
+// the kernel's writes: always on success, never on failure), and returns
+// errnoErr of an arbitrary errno, nil for 0, as Go's stub does with the
+// kernel's. The limits themselves, the process's state, are not modelled.
+func prlimit1(pid int, resource int, newlimit *Rlimit, old *Rlimit) (err error) {
+	if newlimit != nil {
+		_ = *newlimit
+	}
+	if old != nil && gomodel.Choose() {
+		old.Cur = gomodel.ChooseUint64()
+		old.Max = gomodel.ChooseUint64()
+	}
+	e1 := Errno(gomodel.ChooseUint64())
+	if e1 != 0 {
+		err = errnoErr(e1)
+	}
+	return
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go b/goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..d5cc25921
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/time/sleep.go
@@ -0,0 +1,280 @@
+// The Go model of package time's timers (Perennial/Code/time.toml, [model]),
+// spliced into sleep.go in place of the real declarations: the runtime's
+// bodyless newTimer, stopTimer and resetTimer, and NewTimer and After. The
+// rest of sleep.go (Timer.Stop, Timer.Reset, AfterFunc, when, goFunc) is the
+// real Go, translated.
+//
+// A model timer is a goroutine that waits until the runtime clock
+// (runtimeNano) reaches the timer's when and then fires it (startTimer). The
+// runtime's state of a timer, which Go keeps after the Timer in the runtime's
+// allocation (runtime.timeTimer) where no Go type names it, is the primitive
+// stubs newRuntimeTimer, newRuntimeTicker and runtimeTimerFire
+// (TrustedCode/time.lean), whose gc bodies keep it after the Timer as the
+// runtime does. Sleep, a primitive stub too, is a no-op in GooseLang and the
+// runtime's sleep in gc, so gc builds with the overlay keep real-time timers.
+// In GooseLang every clock reading is arbitrary, so a timer may fire at any
+// time after it is started: the model has every schedule of Go's timers.
+
+package time
+
+import (
+	"internal/gomodel"
+	"sync/atomic"
+	"unsafe"
+)
+
+// MODEL: the runtime's newTimer (runtime/time.go) allocates a timer
+// (runtime.timeTimer: the Timer's fields, then the runtime's state), sets
+// initTimer, and arms it: once the runtime clock reaches when it calls
+// f(arg, seq, delay) from the timer goroutine, and again every period if
+// period != 0. The arming (timer.modify) throws if when <= 0 or period < 0;
+// the model is stuck there (startTimer). Its channel argument cp only makes the
+// timer's channel synchronous; NewTimer's and NewTicker's models pass nil and
+// choose the channel's kind themselves (makeTimerChan), and AfterFunc passes
+// nil. The model calls f with arbitrary seq and delay, so it covers the
+// runtime's.
+func newTimer(when, period int64, f func(any, uintptr, int64), arg any, cp unsafe.Pointer) *Timer {
+	fire := timerFunc(f, arg)
+	checkWhen(when, period)
+	t := newRuntimeTimer(fire)
+	startTimer(fire, when, period)
+	return t
+}
+
+// MODEL: the runtime's stopTimer stops the runtime's timer t and reports
+// whether it stopped it before it fired. The model loads t's runtime state
+// (stuck if t is not one of the runtime's timers: a Timer the program
+// allocated, or a copy, where Go reads memory that is not a timer) and returns
+// an arbitrary bool; it does not stop the timer, whose firings may still
+// happen (each one is a schedule of Go's in which Stop came later).
+func stopTimer(t *Timer) bool {
+	return stopTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t))
+}
+
+// MODEL: the runtime's resetTimer re-arms t (when, period: the arming throws
+// as newTimer's does) and reports whether it was armed. The model loads t's
+// runtime state as stopTimer does, is stuck where Go throws, starts one more
+// firing goroutine with the state's call (startTimer) and returns an arbitrary
+// bool. The earlier firings may still happen (as if Reset came later).
+func resetTimer(t *Timer, when, period int64) bool {
+	return resetTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t), when, period)
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's NewTimer is
+//
+//	c := make(chan Time, 1)
+//	t := newTimer(when(d), 0, sendTime, c, syncTimer(c))
+//	t.C = c
+//	return t
+//
+// syncTimer(c) is non-nil unless GODEBUG asynctimerchan=1, and then the
+// runtime makes c synchronous: cap(c) and len(c) are 0, and a receive after a
+// firing gets a value made at the receive. GooseLang cannot change a channel's
+// kind after make, so the model makes c either way (makeTimerChan), and fires
+// sendTimes in place of sendTime: sendTime's non-blocking send would drop the
+// value on an unbuffered c, where the runtime delivers it at the next receive.
+// sendTimes sends blocking, so a receive gets the value whenever it comes (or
+// a later one: the model also allows values after Stop or Reset, which Go's
+// synchronous channels never deliver).
+func NewTimer(d Duration) *Timer {
+	c := makeTimerChan()
+	t := newTimer(when(d), 0, sendTimes, c, nil)
+	t.C = c
+	return t
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's After(d) is NewTimer(d).C: a timer that is never stopped or
+// reset, whose channel delivers one value once the runtime clock reaches
+// when(d). The model is NewTimer's (makeTimerChan, a firing that sends
+// blocking, sendTimes), over the model's runtime clock (runtimeClock), which
+// the proofs can speak of: when(d) is computed (whenAt) from a reading of that
+// clock, and the firing goroutine waits until the clock reaches it
+// (runtimeClockWaitUntil). The model's claim on the clock: the readings of
+// Go's runtime clock form a non-decreasing sequence of positive int64s
+// (runtimeNano), and the model's clock has every such sequence; Go's timer
+// fires only once the clock has reached when.
+func After(d Duration) <-chan Time {
+	c := makeTimerChan()
+	w := whenAt(runtimeClockNow(), d)
+	checkWhen(w, 0)
+	go func() {
+		runtimeClockWaitUntil(w)
+		sendTimes(c, 0, 0)
+	}()
+	return c
+}
+
+// makeTimerChan is a timer's channel: synchronous (GODEBUG asynctimerchan=0,
+// Go's default, whose cap and len are 0) or of capacity 1 (asynctimerchan=1).
+// gomodel.Choose: the model does not read GODEBUG.
+//
+// The kind is fixed here, at make. Go's runtime instead re-reads the setting
+// on every cap and len of a timer channel (and at each send), so a program
+// that changes GODEBUG while running (os.Setenv("GODEBUG",
+// "asynctimerchan=1")) sees cap(t.C) of one timer change from 0 to 1, which
+// no channel of the model does (docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md, "time"). This is
+// sound only because the model's runtime-provided setenv hook is stuck on the
+// key "GODEBUG": no verified program changes the setting mid-run, and with a
+// fixed setting every channel the runtime makes has one kind throughout, which
+// is one of the two choices here (as TrustedCode/crypto/fips140.lean fixes its
+// GODEBUG setting for the whole run).
+func makeTimerChan() chan Time {
+	if gomodel.Choose() {
+		return make(chan Time, 1)
+	}
+	return make(chan Time)
+}
+
+// sendTimes is the model's sendTime: Go's sends Now().Add(Duration(-delta))
+// on c without blocking; this one blocks (see NewTimer).
+func sendTimes(c any, seq uintptr, delta int64) {
+	c.(chan Time) <- Now().Add(Duration(-delta))
+}
+
+// timerFunc is a timer's firing: f(arg, seq, delay) with arbitrary seq and
+// delay (gomodel.ChooseUint64: the runtime's sequence number and lateness).
+func timerFunc(f func(any, uintptr, int64), arg any) func() {
+	return func() {
+		f(arg, uintptr(gomodel.ChooseUint64()), int64(gomodel.ChooseUint64()))
+	}
+}
+
+// checkWhen is stuck where the runtime's timer.modify throws ("timer when
+// must be positive", "timer period must be non-negative").
+func checkWhen(when, period int64) {
+	if when <= 0 || period < 0 {
+		gomodel.Stuck()
+	}
+}
+
+// startTimer arms a timer: a goroutine that waits until the runtime clock
+// reaches when, fires, and for period != 0 does so again at the next when.
+func startTimer(fire func(), when, period int64) {
+	go func() {
+		w := when
+		for {
+			sleepUntil(w)
+			fire()
+			if period == 0 {
+				return
+			}
+			w = nextWhen(w, period)
+		}
+	}()
+}
+
+// nextWhen is a periodic timer's next when: when + period (saturated, as
+// `when` saturates), or now if that has passed already (the runtime skips the
+// missed periods; a value for each would only queue up on the channel).
+func nextWhen(when, period int64) int64 {
+	w := when + period
+	if w < 0 {
+		w = 1<<63 - 1
+	}
+	if now := runtimeNano(); w < now {
+		w = now
+	}
+	return w
+}
+
+// sleepUntil returns once the runtime clock has reached w.
+func sleepUntil(w int64) {
+	for {
+		now := runtimeNano()
+		if w <= now {
+			return
+		}
+		Sleep(Duration(w - now))
+	}
+}
+
+// stopTimerAt is stopTimer at the timer (or ticker) at p.
+func stopTimerAt(p unsafe.Pointer) bool {
+	_ = runtimeTimerFire(p)
+	return gomodel.Choose()
+}
+
+// resetTimerAt is resetTimer at the timer (or ticker) at p.
+func resetTimerAt(p unsafe.Pointer, when, period int64) bool {
+	fire := runtimeTimerFire(p)
+	checkWhen(when, period)
+	startTimer(fire, when, period)
+	return gomodel.Choose()
+}
+
+// newRuntimeTimer is a primitive stub (TrustedCode/time.lean): a new *Timer
+// with initTimer set, followed by the runtime's state of the timer, which
+// holds its firing; GooseLang keeps that state in a cell that no Go
+// allocation has (so runtimeTimerFire is stuck at a Timer the program
+// allocated). In gc the state follows the Timer in one allocation, as the
+// runtime's timeTimer does.
+func newRuntimeTimer(fire func()) *Timer {
+	type timeTimer struct {
+		Timer
+		fire func()
+	}
+	rt := &timeTimer{Timer: Timer{initTimer: true}, fire: fire}
+	return &rt.Timer
+}
+
+// newRuntimeTicker is newRuntimeTimer for a *Ticker (initTicker set), which
+// the runtime allocates as a Timer (Ticker's fields are Timer's).
+func newRuntimeTicker(fire func()) *Ticker {
+	type timeTicker struct {
+		Ticker
+		fire func()
+	}
+	rt := &timeTicker{Ticker: Ticker{initTicker: true}, fire: fire}
+	return &rt.Ticker
+}
+
+// runtimeTimerFire is a primitive stub (TrustedCode/time.lean): the firing
+// held by the runtime's state of the timer at p, made by newRuntimeTimer or
+// newRuntimeTicker (GooseLang: stuck at any other p).
+func runtimeTimerFire(p unsafe.Pointer) func() {
+	type timeTimer struct {
+		Timer
+		fire func()
+	}
+	return (*timeTimer)(p).fire
+}
+
+// runtimeClock is the model's runtime clock (After): its latest reading,
+// which no Go code but runtimeClockNow reads or writes.
+var runtimeClock int64
+
+// runtimeClockNow reads the runtime clock and advances runtimeClock to the
+// reading, unless another reading got there first (then it reads again), so
+// that its results are non-decreasing across goroutines.
+func runtimeClockNow() int64 {
+	for {
+		c := atomic.LoadInt64(&runtimeClock)
+		n := runtimeNano()
+		if c <= n && atomic.CompareAndSwapInt64(&runtimeClock, c, n) {
+			return n
+		}
+	}
+}
+
+// runtimeClockWaitUntil returns a reading of the runtime clock at least w.
+func runtimeClockWaitUntil(w int64) int64 {
+	for {
+		x := runtimeClockNow()
+		if w <= x {
+			return x
+		}
+		Sleep(Duration(w - x))
+	}
+}
+
+// whenAt is when(d) with now for runtimeNano().
+func whenAt(now int64, d Duration) int64 {
+	if d <= 0 {
+		return now
+	}
+	t := now + int64(d)
+	if t < 0 {
+		t = 1<<63 - 1
+	}
+	return t
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/time/tick.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/time/tick.go b/goose/model/overlay/time/tick.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..58ca77549
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/time/tick.go
@@ -0,0 +1,75 @@
+// The Go model of package time's tickers (Perennial/Code/time.toml, [model]),
+// spliced into tick.go in place of the real declarations: NewTicker and
+// Ticker's Stop and Reset, whose Go converts between *Timer and *Ticker
+// through unsafe.Pointer (the semantics converts an unsafe.Pointer only to a
+// pointer to a plain type, Golang/Defn/Plain.lean). The timers are
+// sleep.go's models.
+
+package time
+
+import "unsafe"
+
+// MODEL: Go's NewTicker is
+//
+//	if d <= 0 {
+//		panic("non-positive interval for NewTicker")
+//	}
+//	c := make(chan Time, 1)
+//	t := (*Ticker)(unsafe.Pointer(newTimer(when(d), int64(d), sendTime, c, syncTimer(c))))
+//	t.C = c
+//	return t
+//
+// The model keeps the panic and is NewTimer's model otherwise (the channel,
+// sendTimes), with the runtime's timer allocated as a Ticker
+// (newRuntimeTicker) in place of the conversion, and armed with period d.
+func NewTicker(d Duration) *Ticker {
+	if d <= 0 {
+		panic("non-positive interval for NewTicker")
+	}
+	c := makeTimerChan()
+	w := when(d)
+	fire := timerFunc(sendTimes, c)
+	checkWhen(w, int64(d))
+	t := newRuntimeTicker(fire)
+	startTimer(fire, w, int64(d))
+	t.C = c
+	return t
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's Stop is
+//
+//	if !t.initTicker {
+//		return
+//	}
+//	stopTimer((*Timer)(unsafe.Pointer(t)))
+//
+// The model is the same with stopTimer's model at t's address (stopTimerAt)
+// in place of the conversion, which is the identity on the address.
+func (t *Ticker) Stop() {
+	if !t.initTicker {
+		return
+	}
+	stopTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t))
+}
+
+// MODEL: Go's Reset is
+//
+//	if d <= 0 {
+//		panic("non-positive interval for Ticker.Reset")
+//	}
+//	if !t.initTicker {
+//		panic("time: Reset called on uninitialized Ticker")
+//	}
+//	resetTimer((*Timer)(unsafe.Pointer(t)), when(d), int64(d))
+//
+// The model is the same with resetTimer's model at t's address
+// (resetTimerAt) in place of the conversion.
+func (t *Ticker) Reset(d Duration) {
+	if d <= 0 {
+		panic("non-positive interval for Ticker.Reset")
+	}
+	if !t.initTicker {
+		panic("time: Reset called on uninitialized Ticker")
+	}
+	resetTimerAt(unsafe.Pointer(t), when(d), int64(d))
+}
```

### goose/model/overlay/time/time.go  (tier 0, go-model-overlay)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlay/time/time.go b/goose/model/overlay/time/time.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..e526eb8ea
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlay/time/time.go
@@ -0,0 +1,46 @@
+// The Go model of the runtime's clock readings of package time
+// (Perennial/Code/time.toml, [model]), spliced into time.go in place of the
+// bodyless declarations the runtime defines by linkname. They read the clock
+// through now (time.go, also defined by the runtime), a primitive stub
+// (TrustedCode/time.lean: arbitrary readings), which gc links to the runtime's
+// real clock as before.
+
+package time
+
+import "internal/gomodel"
+
+// MODEL: the runtime's runtimeNow (runtime/time.go, time_runtimeNow) is
+//
+//	if bubble := getg().bubble; bubble != nil {
+//		return bubble.now / 1e9, int32(bubble.now % 1e9), 0
+//	}
+//	return time_now()
+//
+// and time_now is time.now's definition. The model returns now(), whose model
+// is any (sec, nsec, mono); so it covers the bubble's fake clock too.
+func runtimeNow() (sec int64, nsec int32, mono int64) {
+	return now()
+}
+
+// MODEL: the runtime's runtimeNano (time_runtimeNano) is the bubble's fake
+// clock inside a testing/synctest bubble, else nanotime(), the monotonic
+// clock that now() also returns as mono. Both are positive: on Linux nanotime
+// is CLOCK_MONOTONIC, the time since boot, and a bubble's clock starts at
+// 2000-01-01 UTC; `when` relies on it ("runtimeNano() and d are always
+// positive"), and the runtime's timers throw on a non-positive when. The model
+// returns now()'s mono, with a non-positive one (which no Go run reads)
+// replaced by 1, so that its readings are positive as Go's are and the
+// models' timers can keep the runtime's throws (startTimer).
+func runtimeNano() int64 {
+	_, _, mono := now()
+	if mono <= 0 {
+		mono = 1
+	}
+	return mono
+}
+
+// MODEL: the runtime's runtimeIsBubbled is true exactly inside a
+// testing/synctest bubble; the model has both results (gomodel.Choose).
+func runtimeIsBubbled() bool {
+	return gomodel.Choose()
+}
```

### goose/model/overlaytests/cond_test.go  (tier 0, translator)

```diff
diff --git a/goose/model/overlaytests/cond_test.go b/goose/model/overlaytests/cond_test.go
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..fc283dd26
--- /dev/null
+++ b/goose/model/overlaytests/cond_test.go
@@ -0,0 +1,91 @@
+// Package overlaytests holds tests of the Go models (goose/model/overlay) that
+// Go's own tests do not make: behaviours where a model could be more defined
+// than Go. They pass against the real standard library (go test ./...) and
+// under the overlay (etc/model-tests.sh).
+package overlaytests
+
+import (
+	"sync"
+	"testing"
+	"time"
+)
+
+// waiter starts a goroutine that locks mu, waits on c and then closes done,
+// and returns once the goroutine has taken its ticket (Wait has unlocked mu).
+func waiter(mu *sync.Mutex, c *sync.Cond, done chan<- struct{}) {
+	started := make(chan struct{})
+	go func() {
+		mu.Lock()
+		close(started)
+		c.Wait()
+		mu.Unlock()
+		close(done)
+	}()
+	<-started
+	// Wait takes its ticket before unlocking mu: once we hold mu, it has.
+	mu.Lock()
+	mu.Unlock()
+}
+
+// returnsWithin reports whether done is closed within d.
+func returnsWithin(done <-chan struct{}, d time.Duration) bool {
+	select {
+	case <-done:
+		return true
+	case <-time.After(d):
+		return false
+	}
+}
+
+// Cond.Wait has no spurious wakeups: without Signal or Broadcast it does not
+// return (in Go it parks; the model of runtime_notifyListWait spins).
+func TestCondWaitBlocksWithoutSignal(t *testing.T) {
+	var mu sync.Mutex
+	c := sync.NewCond(&mu)
+	done := make(chan struct{})
+	waiter(&mu, c, done)
+	if returnsWithin(done, 300*time.Millisecond) {
+		t.Fatal("Cond.Wait returned without Signal or Broadcast")
+	}
+	c.Signal()
+	if !returnsWithin(done, 10*time.Second) {
+		t.Fatal("Cond.Wait did not return after Signal")
+	}
+}
+
+// Signal wakes one waiter, not two; Broadcast wakes the rest.
+func TestCondSignalWakesOne(t *testing.T) {
+	var mu sync.Mutex
+	c := sync.NewCond(&mu)
+	d1, d2 := make(chan struct{}), make(chan struct{})
+	waiter(&mu, c, d1)
+	waiter(&mu, c, d2)
+	c.Signal()
+	if !returnsWithin(d1, 10*time.Second) {
+		t.Fatal("the first waiter did not return after Signal")
+	}
+	if returnsWithin(d2, 300*time.Millisecond) {
+		t.Fatal("Signal woke two waiters")
+	}
+	c.Broadcast()
+	if !returnsWithin(d2, 10*time.Second) {
+		t.Fatal("the second waiter did not return after Broadcast")
+	}
+}
+
+// A Signal with no waiter is not remembered: a later Wait still blocks.
+func TestCondSignalWithoutWaiterIsLost(t *testing.T) {
+	var mu sync.Mutex
+	c := sync.NewCond(&mu)
+	c.Signal()
+	c.Broadcast()
+	done := make(chan struct{})
+	waiter(&mu, c, done)
+	if returnsWithin(done, 300*time.Millisecond) {
+		t.Fatal("Cond.Wait returned after a Signal that preceded it")
+	}
+	c.Broadcast()
+	if !returnsWithin(done, 10*time.Second) {
+		t.Fatal("Cond.Wait did not return after Broadcast")
+	}
+}
```

```diff
-// Package pool is the Go model of the state of a sync.Pool, used by the
-// trusted sync.(*Pool).Get and sync.(*Pool).Put (Perennial/TrustedCode/sync.lean),
-// which keep a *Pool in the sync.Pool's field local.
-//
-// A Pool is a multiset of the values Put and not yet taken by a Get. It is
-// more nondeterministic than gc's per-P pools, its victim cache and their
-// clearing at each GC, so that it covers them: Put may drop its value, and Get
-// may ignore the pool, even when it is not empty; otherwise Get removes and
-// returns an arbitrary element. Each value Put is returned by at most one Get,
-// as in gc, and the lock makes a Put of x synchronize before the Get returning
-// x, as the sync.Pool documentation promises.
-package pool
-
-import (
-	"github.com/goose-lang/primitive"
-)
-
-// Pool is the state of a sync.Pool. Its zero value is an empty pool.
-type Pool struct {
-	mu    primitive.Mutex
-	items []any
-}
-
-// Put adds x to the pool, or drops it. x is not nil (sync.(*Pool).Put ignores
-// nil).
-func (p *Pool) Put(x any) {
-	if primitive.RandomUint64() == 0 {
-		return
-	}
-	p.mu.Lock()
-	p.items = append(p.items, x)
-	p.mu.Unlock()
-}
-
-// Get removes an arbitrary element of the pool and returns it, or returns nil
-// (even when the pool is not empty).
-func (p *Pool) Get() any {
-	p.mu.Lock()
-	n := uint64(len(p.items))
-	if n == 0 || primitive.RandomUint64() == 0 {
-		p.mu.Unlock()
-		return nil
-	}
-	i := primitive.RandomUint64() % n
-	x := p.items[i]
-	p.items[i] = p.items[n-1]
-	p.items = p.items[:n-1]
-	p.mu.Unlock()
-	return x
-}
```
